Amended by D-692 (UX-17, the owner to confirm): after "Load demo data" the dashboard opens "Past year" (`/?period=past-year`), no longer the demo's whole period.
- D-522 (the owner; the details the PM's). Entries' periods are the dashboard's: "All time" (Entries' default, no dates), the dashboard's presets in its order and with its labels (`PRESETS`, `PERIOD_LABELS` and `presetRange` from one place that both pages use), then "Custom" (disabled, shown chosen for typed dates). Entries' own "Last month" and "Last year" go. The URL keeps a preset by name (`period=<preset>`), `from` and `to` stay for Custom, and the list's requests send the preset's `from` and `to`. Typing From or To turns the period into Custom and keeps the other end. "Month of the latest entry" here is the whole ledger's, whatever the other filters. Links into Entries from Accounts, Categories and the dashboard stay as they are and open All time.
- D-523 (the owner; the details the PM's). An entry's family record's payee. An entry a family budget posted, such as a member's share, has no payee of its own, so its Payee cell showed "—" and the payee filter, the text search and the summary missed it. The entry view's `family` gets `recordPayee` (`{ name }`; additive, left out otherwise, display only, nothing stored, rule 13): the name of the payee on the payer's own payment entry of the entry's record, exactly when the reader may see that payee (D-479 to D-482, D-486): the reader is an ACTIVE member of that budget, and is the payer, or the payer is an ACTIVE member with an account who shows their payees there, the rule `paymentPayee` and `FamilyPostingService.shownPayees` follow. For EXPENSE and INCOME records, refunds included; never for a settlement, an opening balance or a correction. One SQL rule serves the entry view, the search and the summary. The Payee cell shows the entry's own payee, else its lines' counterparties, else `recordPayee`, styled as any payee; the entry's page (`ReadOnlyEntry`) shows it too. `q` matches it as it matches a payee's name; the Payee filter (`counterpartyId`) matches an entry with no payee and no counterparty on its lines when its `recordPayee` has the chosen counterparty's name, ignoring case. A hidden payee never shows, matches or counts anywhere, for any reader. Who sees a payee doesn't change, only where; the privacy policy and `payees.ts` say what members see, not where (D-541).
- D-524 (the owner; the details the PM's). Entries' summary row, right under the column headers, covering every entry the filters match on every page: Date, the number of entries and the first and last date ("214 entries · 1–31 Oct 2026", `formatRange`); Payee, the distinct payees as the cells show them (names compared ignoring case) and "n without"; Category, the distinct categories as the cells show them (the categorized lines', else the record's, D-477) and "n without"; Accounts, the distinct accounts the column names (the lines without a category, FX_EXCHANGE left out where `describeEntry` leaves it out); Amount, per currency as the rows show amounts: "In +X" (rows shown with +), "Out −Y" (rows shown with −), "Net" only with both, "Transfers Z" (rows without a sign), each only when not 0, never added across currencies. `GET /api/entries/summary` takes the list's filters, its WHERE built once for both endpoints, read only in one snapshot; the page computes the net with `money.ts`. It is asked when the filters change, not the page; while it reloads the old figures stay, dimmed; under 40rem the row is a block of labelled lines. The row's sign and amount rule now lives twice, in `describeEntry` and in the summary's SQL: the backend tests cover each kind, and the end-to-end spec compares the row with the cells.
- D-525 (the owner; the details the PM's). Accounts' columns line up: Assets, Liabilities and Equity show their columns at the same widths and left edges at every width from 40rem, with and without a comparison: one table, a `tbody` per type with a row for its name and hint and a row for its total, the type names headings that label their groups. Under 40rem each account stays a wrapping block (D-382, D-386), and nothing scrolls sideways at 375 px.
- D-526 (the owner; the details the PM's). Accounts on a date, and compared: "Balances on" a `DateInput`, the user's today by default (`on=`, left out for today), and "Compare", which adds "Compared with" (`compare=`, by default the last day of the month before the first date's month) and a button that ends the comparison. Data: `/api/reports/balances?asOf=<day>` for each day and "By person" from `/api/reports/counterparty-balances?…&asOf=<day>`; no new endpoint. In a comparison the columns come in date order, whichever date was typed first: the earlier day's balance, the later day's, "Change" (later − earlier, the PM's addition) and "%" against the earlier day (`dashboard.changeOf` as it is, not the net's rule: "—" across 0 and from 0), per currency, for every account, each type's total and "By person"; the headers name the days in the user's date format; no colour for better or worse. An account is listed while it is listed on either day (`listedInBalances`), or with "Show archived". Rename, Archive and Restore stay; archiving still looks at every posting (D-366).
- D-527 (the owner; the details the PM's). Icon buttons on the Categories page: Rename, Archive and Restore become buttons with a pencil, a box with an arrow down and a box with an arrow up, and "Change it in the family budget" a link with an arrow leaving a box; each square, 36px (44px under 40rem), its accessible name and `title` the action and the category ("Rename Groceries", "Change Groceries in the family budget Home"). Inline SVG in a new `icons.tsx`, `currentColor` and `aria-hidden`, as `SharedIcon`; no new dependency. The rename form's Save and Cancel keep their words, Archive's confirmation stays; Accounts and a family budget's categories keep their words.
- D-528 (the PM). What UX-11 doesn't do: no migration (Flyway stays at V17); no change to a report's figures, the dashboard's blocks (the strip is UX-12), import and export, New entry's similar entries or the family budget's pages; no decision number in any screen's text (D-353) or the API's messages (D-384).
- D-529 (the agent, UX-11). `RecordPayees` (`ledger/`) is D-523's one rule: a `LEFT JOIN LATERAL` on the entry `e` that gives `rp.name`, with the reader's sub and the switch as parameters. `EntryService.families` joins it for `family.recordPayee`, and `EntryService.matching` builds the FROM and WHERE of both `search` and `summary` with it. It applies to every non-detached SHARE or PAYMENT link of an EXPENSE or INCOME record, so the payer's own payment entry gets it too, equal to the entry's own payee; the cell shows the entry's own first, so nothing changes there. A settlement's sides (link SETTLEMENT), an opening balance and a correction (no record) never match.
- D-530 (the agent, UX-11). With the family switch off, `recordPayee` is never given and never matched (the parameter `rpOn` is false), as `paymentPayee` isn't served then; the entry's `family` itself is unchanged (`FamilySwitchOffApiTests`).
- D-531 (the agent, UX-11). `q` matches `rp.name` like the entry's payee's name, whatever else the entry has; the Payee filter matches it only for an entry with no payee and no counterparty on its lines, as D-523 says, so that both find exactly what the cells show. Names are compared with PostgreSQL's `lower()`.
- D-532 (the agent, UX-11). The summary's answer (`EntrySummary`): `count`, `firstDate`, `lastDate` (null for none), `payees`, `withoutPayee`, `categories`, `withoutCategory`, `accounts`, and `currencies`, a list sorted by currency of `{currency, incoming, outgoing, transfers}` as decimal strings at or above 0 (`in` is a Java keyword). Payees are counted per name, so a cell listing two counterparties counts both; categories and accounts by id, so a personal and a family category of one name count twice; FX_EXCHANGE is the ledger's account of that code. The sign follows `describeEntry` exactly: the net of an entry's categorized lines summed across its currencies, below 0 In, above 0 Out, else Transfers, and per currency the sum of its positive lines.
- D-533 (the agent, UX-11). UX-9's tests that said a payee never reaches the other members' own entries (`FamilyPayeeApiTests.bobNeverSeesItElsewhere`, `DataIsolationApiTests.aPayeeIsShownOnlyByItsPayersChoice`) now allow it there only as the share's `family.recordPayee` while the payer shows payees, and still find it nowhere else; hidden, they find it nowhere, as before.
- D-534 (the agent, UX-11). `periods.ts` holds `PRESETS`, `PERIOD_LABELS`, `presetRange`, `presetNamed`, `validDate` and `useLatestEntryDay`; `dashboard.ts` re-exports the first ones. `presetRange` takes the latest entry's day for `latest-entry-month`, and `periodFromQuery` and `needsLatestEntry` read the URL. The dashboard shows "Loading the dashboard" until that day is known, and only for that preset; the answer is kept while another preset is chosen, so coming back doesn't wait again. An unknown `period` is the month of the latest entry on the dashboard (D-521) and All time on Entries, each page's default.
- D-535 (the agent, UX-11). On Entries a valid `period` wins over `from` and `to`; an old Entries link whose `from` and `to` equal a preset's days now reads as Custom, since presets are kept by name only (Entries never wrote `period` before).
- D-536 (the agent, UX-11). The summary row is a `tbody.entry-summary` between the header and the entries, named "Summary of all matching entries", each cell with a label shown only under 40rem; its In amounts in `--income`, Out in `--expense` as the rows' tones; Net signed unless 0; the dates through `formatDayRange` in the browser's locale, as the dashboard's range names (`rangeLabel`), not the user's date format. `describeEntry`'s payee takes `recordPayee` as its last fallback, so the cell, the entry's page and every other user of it read the same.
- D-537 (the agent, UX-11). Accounts: every balances request names its day (`asOf`), today's too; "Compare" writes the default `compare=` into the URL; the button that ends it is "End the comparison"; equal days give two equal columns; a comparison cell under 40rem names its column (`data-label`), the headers being hidden there; "Change" is signed (`+€10.00`, `€0.00`), "%" by `changeText`; a currency missing on a day shows "—" there, the change counting it as 0; "By person" is a nested table compared as the accounts are, not aligned with the outer columns.
- D-538 (the agent, UX-11). `icons.tsx`: `PencilIcon`, `ArchiveIcon`, `RestoreIcon` and `ExternalIcon`, 16-unit strokes in `currentColor`; the family link is an `<a class="button icon-button">`.
- D-539 (the agent, UX-11). End to end: D-523's check extends `09-payees` (B's share on Entries names A's payee while A shows payees, "—" otherwise, and `q` finds it only then), its roles being A the payer and B the reader; the Categories check is the last step of `11-entries`, on the same demo; `12-accounts` compares figures as numbers in ten-thousandths against `/api/reports/balances` and works out "%" in the test; the columns' check measures each type's first account row, at 1280 and 800 px, and doesn't run at 375 px (`local-narrow`), where each account is a block.
- D-540 (the agent, UX-11). `e2e/scripts/screens.ts` (D-513) also takes Entries for a month, a family share whose payee comes from its record (a family expense with a payee added to the demo's budget) and Accounts compared. The "before" set of `1edc62c` comes from a scratch worktree's Vite on another port against the same local backend.
- D-541 (the agent, UX-11; D-523's question). Neither the privacy policy nor `payees.ts` lists the places where a member sees a payee: both say what is seen (the payee's name, while the payer shows it), so neither changes.
- D-542 (the agent, UX-11). `deploy/checks/UX-11.sql` and `UX-11.expected` are UX-10's structure check (V17's column, check and trigger, and the highest migration 17) under UX-11's name, since UX-11 changes no schema.
- D-543 (the agent, UX-11; D-520's report). `npm audit` in `frontend/`: 4 advisories (2 moderate, 2 high), all dev only (`npm audit --omit=dev`: 0), none in the web image's bundle: `vite` 8.3.0 (high, direct; the dev server and launch-editor), `undici` 7.30.0 (high, through `jsdom`), `vitest` 5.0.2 and `@vitest/mocker` 5.0.2 (moderate, direct and through `vitest`); each fixed by `npm audit fix` without `--force` (vite 8.3.8, undici 7.30.2, vitest 5.1.0). Its dry run also removes `@rolldown/binding-linux-x64-musl` from the lock file, which the build image (`node:24-alpine`, musl) needs for `npm ci`: a fix made on the laptop must keep it. `e2e/`: 0 either way.
- D-544 (the agent, UX-11). New entry's similar entries (UX-8) show `describeEntry`'s payee too, so a family share listed there would read its record's payee; the matches never list a share (D-457), so nothing changes there.
- D-545 (the PM). Stage names: this stage is UX-12; D-503's five-figure strip and daily spending become UX-13 (D-518 amended).
- D-546 (the PM). Deploys. As D-519, UX-12 records none of UX-8's to UX-11's deploys: "Production now", "Deployed revisions" and CLAUDE.md's Deployment line stay as they are; after the review the PM sends their record, from the server's run summaries, as this stage's second commit. UX-11's change log entry says under Open that its deploy waits for UX-10's record; it didn't: UX-11 was deployed on 2026-11-12 (`136e5c2`, its `finish` at 2026-11-12T12:57:22Z, Flyway V17, family budgets on; rollback target `1edc62c`, no condition), and the records wait. UX-12's entry says so; the old entry stays as it is.
- D-547 (the owner; the details the PM's). Entries' "Payee or person" lists every payee the page can show: one list sorted by name ignoring case, of the user's counterparties as before (archived ones marked, each choosing `counterpartyId`) and D-548's names that no counterparty of the user's has, ignoring case, each labelled "<name> · <budget>" (its family budgets by name, joined with ", ") and choosing `payee=<name>`. A counterparty and a family name equal ignoring case are one option, the counterparty, which already finds both (D-531). Choosing one filter drops the other from the URL. The chosen value is always listed, also when D-548 no longer returns it (the payer hid payees since), then as the name alone. `payee` is a filter like the others: the summary row gets it (D-524), "Clear filters" clears it, a change starts at the first page.
- D-548 (the PM). The backend. `GET /api/entries/family-payees`, read only: the distinct names of `family.recordPayee` over the user's personal ledger's entries, by `RecordPayees`' rule, names compared with `lower()` (D-531), each with the family budgets it comes from (`id`, `name`); sorted by name ignoring case; `[]` with the switch off or without family links; no paging; nothing stored. `payee` on `GET /api/entries` and `/summary`: a name, trimmed, blank as absent, at most 100 characters (else 400); the entries whose Payee cell shows it: the entry's payee's name, or a line's counterparty's name, or for an entry with neither its shown `recordPayee`, all by `lower()`. With `counterpartyId` as well: 400. Built in `EntryService.matching`, so the list and the summary can't disagree; with the switch off only the first two match. `counterpartyId` and everything else unchanged.
- D-549 (the owner; the details the PM's). The payee on the family forms is typed freely, with suggestions: `CounterpartyFields`' payee, so the new expense or income (`FamilyExpenseForm.tsx`), the record page's form (`EditRecord`) and the payment entry (`PaymentEntry.tsx`); the account's required counterparty (D-80) stays a list, and a settlement still has no payee. The field is `CounterpartyInput` (a text input with a datalist), opened with the current payee's name; the suggestions the user's counterparties not archived and D-548's names, one per name ignoring case, sorted; the hint as before (`payeeHint`), plus "New payee: it is added when you save." for a name no counterparty of the user's has. On save, by the trimmed name: empty removes the payee (`payeeId: null`) if there was one; the payee it had (ignoring case): nothing sent; a counterparty of the user's (archived included): its id; any other name: the user's counterparty is created first (`POST /api/counterparties`) and its id sent, remembered for a second save before the list reloads (D-462); a failure after the creation leaves the counterparty and shows the error as before. A name from the partner's payees becomes the user's own counterparty, in his ledger only, and stays his if the partner later hides his payees. Typing changes which payee the record has; it never renames an existing counterparty.
- D-550 (the PM). The personal forms' `PayeeField` (the entry's own payee) also suggests D-548's names, deduplicated as in D-549; a chosen one is created on save as any new name is. Fields for a line's counterparty (a loan's person) don't.
- D-551 (the PM, on D-543). In `frontend/`: `npm audit fix` without `--force` (vite, undici, vitest, @vitest/mocker). `@rolldown/binding-linux-x64-musl` stays in `package-lock.json` (D-543); if the fix drops it, it is put back with nothing else changed. Afterwards `npm audit` in `frontend/` and `e2e/` both 0, and the web image built locally (`docker build --target web`, a tag of the agent's own, removed by name afterwards), whose `npm ci` proves the lock. No other dependency changes; `e2e/` untouched.
- D-552 (the PM, on UX-11's review). Two layout fixes, nothing else on those pages. Accounts compared, under 40rem: each account's figures under its name in a grid of two columns, the earlier and the later day's balance on the first line, Change and % on the second, each named by its `data-label`; the type totals and "By person" the same; at 1280 px as before. Categories: the name vertically centred on the line of its icon buttons, at 1280 and 375 px, on `Categories.tsx` and a family budget's categories alike.
- D-553 (the PM). What UX-12 doesn't do: no migration (Flyway stays at V17); no change to who sees a payee, nor to the privacy policy (as D-541: what a member sees is unchanged), to a report's figure, to import and export, to New entry's similar entries; no decision number in a screen's text (D-353) or the API's messages (D-384).
- D-554 (the agent, UX-12). `payee`'s 100 characters are counted after trimming, in `EntryFilter`'s constructor (an `IllegalArgumentException`, 400 like the filter's other refusals), not by `@Size` on the raw parameter. A family payee's one spelling is the one on the newest entry that shows it (entry date, then id, descending); its budgets come sorted by name. `RecordPayees`' join also gives `family_ledger_id`, the record's budget, which only `familyPayees` reads. The endpoint is not behind the family switch's 404: with it off it answers `[]`, as D-548 says.
- D-555 (the agent, UX-12; finding). `DataIsolationApiTests.aReturnsOwnEntriesAndAClaimedSeatAreTheMembersOwn` failed once with the new classes in the shared database: it looked for Alice's account id among every number of Bob's lookup, and ids of different tables (a category's, a member's) come from different sequences and may be equal. It now looks only in fields that name an account (`Answers.numbers(answer, field)`, new); a test-isolation fault of D-99's kind, fixed, not pinned.
- D-556 (the agent, UX-12). A URL with both `counterpartyId` and `payee` (only by hand) is read as the counterparty on Entries: the page sends no `payee` then, so the backend's 400 for both never shows.
- D-557 (the agent, UX-12; finding). `11-entries` raced twice in UX-12's runs, as in UX-11's first: `allTextContents()` read Entries' Period list before the page had rendered, and step 2's response waits then took step 1's still-pending All-time answer (164 entries) for the previous month's. Step 1 now waits for the list, its rows and its summary, and for the network to go idle.
- D-558 (the agent, UX-12). `familyPayees.ts` holds `useFamilyPayees` (asked only with the family switch on; nothing while it loads or after a failure), Entries' options (`payeeFilterOptions`, `payeeFilterValue`, `payeeFilterOf`) and the save rule (`payeeChange`, `payeeIdFor`, `NEW_PAYEE_HINT`); `payeeNames.ts`, without React or `me.tsx`, the name helpers `CounterpartyInput` uses (`payeeSuggestions`, `counterpartyCalled`), so that `components.tsx` imports no hook module. `CounterpartyInput` takes `familyPayees` (none by default), so a loan's person field suggests as before.
- D-559 (the agent, UX-12). The record page's payee field opens empty until the reader's counterparties have loaded, then with the current payee's name; once typed, the text stays across a failed save. A new payer (the payer changed to the reader) has no payee yet, so whatever is typed is sent. On the new record form the payee goes only with the reader's own payment, as before. After a payment entry's save, New entry's page reloads the counterparties too, so a payee created there opens by its name.
- D-560 (the agent, UX-12; D-551's result). `npm audit fix` (npm 11.6.2, Node 24.21) changed `package-lock.json` only, `package.json`'s ranges allowing the new versions: `vite` 8.3.0 → 8.3.8, `undici` 7.30.0 → 7.30.2, `vitest`, `@vitest/mocker` and `@vitest/spy` 5.0.2 → 5.1.0, `rolldown` and its bindings 1.2.9 → 1.2.19, and other transitive updates (`postcss` 8.5.32, `chai` 6.3.0 and the like), with `@rolldown/binding-wasm32-wasi` and its `@emnapi`, `@napi-rs`, `@tybys` and `tslib` packages added as optional entries. `@rolldown/binding-linux-x64-musl` stayed (1.2.19), so nothing was put back; D-543's dry run had read otherwise.
- D-561 (the agent, UX-12). `deploy/checks/UX-12.sql` and `UX-12.expected` are UX-11's check with two invariants the payee filter reads by name: no entry whose payee, and no posting whose counterparty, is of another ledger than the entry's.

- D-569 (the owner, D-495, D-503; the details the PM's). The headline strip: five figures, as design C. Order: You have, You owe (or "Owed to you", D-350), Net worth, Income, Expenses. The first three as before (D-301, as of the balances' day, the whole ledger); Income and Expenses the period's totals that `PeriodTotals` showed (D-305), each with D-400's change line, and they move: the card "Income and expenses" no longer shows the totals; the period's net stays the cash flow table's Net row. Income and Expenses follow "Show" and "Records"; with any choice but All a muted line under each names it as the controls do ("Personal", "Demo household · My records"). In the base currency one strip, the exchange results small under Net worth and the rates' line under the strip; in each currency one strip per currency, its label as before, Income and Expenses from that currency's table, a figure a currency doesn't have "—". The first three never wait for the cash flow, nor the last two for net worth: each part shows its own loading and error. Layout: from 64rem five equal columns in one row, one size, Mono (D-500, D-510); between 40rem and 64rem they wrap, the balance figures first; under 40rem, as design C's phone, Net worth first on its own line and larger, then You have and You owe, then Income and Expenses, two columns. Income `--income`, Expenses `--expense`, the change line as D-401/D-501; no new token. The page's dl/dt/dd semantics stay; the strip is one `section` named "Headline figures". Amended by D-685 (UX-17): Income's and Expenses' change lines leave out a comparison with nothing posted in the currency, as they leave out a "—".
- D-570 (the PM). The daily report, read only, in `ReportService`, one statement per answer, nothing stored (rule 13): `GET /api/reports/daily-cash-flow?from=&to=` with the cash flow's `scope` and `familyLedgerId` and their rules (D-302, D-308, D-421; a family scope with the switch off 400): rows `{day, categoryCode, categoryName, categoryType, currency, total, familyLedgerId?, familyLedgerName?}`, the cash flow's rows with the day in place of the month, by the same classification and the same family categories, so that over any range and scope the days' totals per category and currency add up exactly to `/cash-flow`'s. `currency=BASE` (a second handler on the path): `{currency, rows}`, each row the day's converted `total` (each posting at its own day's rate, rounded once per category and day, null with `missingRates` when a rate is missing) and its `rates`; no exchange results; over a range the converted days may differ from the monthly figures by their rounding only. `from` after `to`, or a range of more than 366 days: 400. Tests: the sums against `/cash-flow` in each currency and every scope, BASE with a missing rate, the 400s, the switch off, another user's ledger never.
- D-571 (the owner, D-503; the details the PM's). Daily spending, as design C. Inside the card "Income and expenses", beside the chart from 64rem (the chart about two thirds), under it below; in each-currency mode one panel per currency, beside that currency's chart. The days: the section's days (D-396) when the period has at most 31 days; otherwise the calendar month of the section's last day, from the period's From if that is later; requested from the first shown day to the earlier of the last and today, with the page's scope and currency mode; days after today, up to the shown range's end, empty dashed slots (`--border`), not requested. Title "Daily spending, November 2026" for a whole calendar month, else "Daily spending, <the range>" by `rangeLabel`/`formatRange`. A day's spending is its EXPENSE rows' total (a refund lowers it, D-79): a bar per day in `--chart-expense`, its height by the shown range's largest day, 72px high (60px under 40rem); a day at or below 0 a 2px `--border` line; a day the base currency can't convert no bar, titled "rate missing", its missing rates in the page's notice. Plain HTML and CSS, no chart library. Under the bars the first day's number, today's while the range runs, and the last day's, Mono, `--muted`; then "Biggest day: <day>, <amount>, <its largest category>" (ties: the earlier day, the category first by name; a family category with its budget as the table names it), or "No spending in this period." when no day is above 0. The bars `aria-hidden`, with a text alternative a screen reader gets in full (a visually hidden table of each shown day with spending and its amount, and the biggest day); each bar's `title` its day and amount. Its own load and error: the chart and the table never wait for it. Amended by D-686 (UX-17): daily spending isn't shown for a currency none of whose shown days has a figure, nor before a day is asked; the chart then takes the row's whole width.

- D-573 (the PM). What UX-13 doesn't do: no migration (Flyway stays at V17); the rest of design C's dashboard stays out (Balances beside the chart, the categories' share bars, a Rates card, a period as buttons); no change to the Balances and Open loans cards, the cash flow table, the chart, Entries or the family budget's pages, to import and export; no decision number in a screen's text (D-353) or the API's messages (D-384).
- D-574 (the agent, UX-13). D-571's days as built (`daily.ts`, `dailyRange`): a period of at most 31 days shows the section's days, so while it runs it ends today and has no slot to come ("Daily spending, Nov 1 – 12, 2026" for this month on the 12th); a longer period shows the month of the section's last day from the period's From when that is later and, the agent's addition, to the period's To when that is earlier, so the panel never shows a day outside the period (a past custom range of 20 August to 15 October shows 1 to 15 October). So the dashed slots appear for a longer running period (a quarter or a year: this month's days after today). Nothing is asked when the first shown day is after today (a period in the future: every slot to come).
- D-575 (the agent, UX-13). D-570's range counts both ends: 366 days answer (a leap year's whole year, or 1 January to 1 January), 367 are a 400, "The daily cash flow covers at most 366 days."; a missing `from` or `to` and any other `currency` are 400 as for `/cash-flow`.
- D-576 (the agent, UX-13). One SQL builder for both reports in each currency (`ReportService.flowRows`, its bucket the month's first day or the day) and one CATEGORY part for both in the base currency (`categorySums`, which `cashFlowInBase` and `dailyCashFlowInBase` share), so the daily report classifies exactly as the cash flow by construction; `/cash-flow`'s statements and answers are unchanged. In the base currency the rows come from one statement and the rates from `RateService.rateBook` (`from` to `to`, with the latest before `from`), in one REPEATABLE READ read-only transaction, as every report in the base currency: "one statement per answer" read as one statement for the figures.
- D-577 (the agent, UX-13). No checklist is added or changed.
- D-613 (the owner). Keycloak's pages take design C: the sign-in pages, the account console and the emails; the realm's displayed name is "Finance Tracker".
- D-614 (the owner). Keycloak's pages show the theme chosen in the app, through a cookie the app writes. With no choice, they follow the device as the app does.
- D-615 (the PM). The theme's shape and how it runs. One theme, `finance-tracker`, in `auth_server/deploy/themes/finance-tracker/`, with `login/`, `account/` and `email/`, each with its `theme.properties`; parents `keycloak.v2` (login), `keycloak.v3` (account) and Keycloak's email theme; the parents' own styles and scripts stay, and ours come after them. The parent themes are read from the pinned image's themes JAR, never from memory or the web, naming the PatternFly version they use. No FreeMarker template is copied for login or account: the look comes from CSS, a small script and messages; for the email only the HTML layout template is replaced (a page that can't be made right that way: stop and ask). IDs and names stay Keycloak's (the app's e2e signs in through `#username`, `#password` and `#kc-login`). Fonts: IBM Plex Sans 400, 500 and 600, latin, latin-ext and cyrillic, `woff2`, with their `unicode-range`, the same files as the app's `@fontsource/ibm-plex-sans` 5.3.0, in the theme's resources with `OFL.txt` beside them; nothing from a CDN. Mounted read-only (`./themes/finance-tracker:/opt/keycloak/themes/finance-tracker:ro`) in `deploy/docker-compose.yml` and the dev stack's `docker-compose.yml`; files 644, directories 755, so that Keycloak (uid 1000) reads them. Production keeps the theme cache on; `sync.sh` restarts `keycloak` when a file under `themes/` changed, as it restarts caddy for the Caddyfile, and says that sign-in is unavailable for about a minute; `--dry-run` says it would; `--copy-only` doesn't restart and says a restart is needed. The dev stack's Keycloak image is production's, tag and digest.
- D-616 (the PM). Which theme a Keycloak page shows. A script in the page's head sets `data-theme` on `<html>` before the first paint: the cookie `ft_theme` when it is `blue`, `light` or `dark`, otherwise the device (dark → `blue`, light or none → `light`), following the device's changes while there is no cookie. The tokens are D-496's 22 values per theme, copied with a comment naming finance's `index.css` as their source, plus `color-scheme`; `keycloak.v2`'s own dark mode isn't used. The account console the same if `keycloak.v3` loads the script; if it can't, it follows the device by CSS alone (dark → Blue's tokens, else Light's); say which.
- D-617 (the PM). The cookie, written by the app (`frontend/src/theme.ts`): `ft_theme=<choice>; Path=/; Max-Age=31536000; SameSite=Lax`, plus `Secure` on https. `Domain` is the parent of the app's host when the host has at least three labels (`app.finance-nl.com` → `finance-nl.com`); with fewer, no `Domain`; on `localhost` a host-only cookie, which Keycloak on `localhost:8080` reads too. Written by `setTheme` on every choice, also when storage refuses it, and by `startTheme` when a stored choice exists (renewing its year and covering choices made before UX-15); never without a choice. Nothing on the server reads it; nothing else of `theme.ts` changes. The privacy policy's "Cookies" list gains it, what it holds and why, with a new date.
- D-618 (the PM). The sign-in pages: page `--bg`; a centred column at most 400px wide, 16px sides under 40rem; on top the realm's name "Finance Tracker" as Keycloak's header, Plex Sans 600, 20px, Keycloak's logo and background image gone; the form in a panel (`--surface`, a 1px `--border` border, radius 8px, padding 28px, 20px 16px under 40rem), the page's `h1` 20/28, 600. Fields: the label 13px `--muted` above; the input 40px high (44px with 16px text under 40rem), `--surface-raised`, a 1px `--border-control` border, radius 6px; the password's show button inside the field's border; an error 13px `--danger` under the field, and the field's border `--danger`. Buttons: the primary full width, 40px, `--accent` with `--on-accent`, 600; Google and GitHub side by side as default buttons (`--surface`, `--border-control`, `--text`), their icons in `currentColor`; "Or sign in with" between two `--border` lines, 12px `--muted`. Links `--accent`: "Forgot Password?" right-aligned under the password, "New user? Register" under the panel. Alerts as the app's notices (error `--danger-bg` with `--danger`, warning `--notice-bg` with `--text`, success `--success-bg` with `--success`, info `--surface-raised` with `--text`). Focus: a 2px `--accent` outline, offset 2px. The texts stay Keycloak's own, in English. Every page the realm can show: sign in, register, forgot password, update password, verify email; the info and error pages (an email verified, a link expired, the page expired) and the logout confirmation; a first sign-in with Google or GitHub (review profile, account exists: link by email or by signing in); set up and enter a one-time code.
- D-619 (the PM). The account console and the way to it. `keycloak.v3` in the tokens and Plex Sans: the masthead `--surface` with "Finance Tracker" as the wordmark in place of Keycloak's logo (by CSS, or an SVG of the text drawn as paths; say which); the navigation, forms, buttons and tables in D-618's look and the app's D-502; the current navigation item `--surface-raised` with a 2px `--accent` line on its left. The app's user menu gains "Account" between Settings and Log out: a plain link, same tab, to the console with `referrer=finance-tracker`, so that the console's "Back to Finance Tracker" returns to the client's base URL; if Keycloak wants a `referrer_uri`, it must match a valid redirect URI: add none, and say what was found. The address comes from the backend, which knows the issuer: an additive field of `/api/me` (`accountUrl`); no Keycloak address in the frontend. Under 40rem it is like the menu's other items.
- D-620 (the PM). The emails. One HTML layout for every email: a 560px panel on `#F4F6F9`, white, a 1px `#D5DCE6` border, radius 8px; a header row with "Finance Tracker", 16px 600; the body 15/23 `#1B2433`; the action as a button (`#5B48D6`, white text, radius 6px), the plain link under it in 13px `#566173`; a footer line, 12px `#566173`. Inline styles; fonts `'IBM Plex Sans', Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif`, no web font loaded; Light's colours only, in every client. The plain-text versions stay Keycloak's. The subjects "Verify your email for Finance Tracker" and "Reset your Finance Tracker password"; the others stay Keycloak's. The bodies are Keycloak's texts, with the realm's name (D-621).
- D-621 (the owner's name; the details the PM's). The realm's settings, which the checklist sets with kcadm: `displayName` = "Finance Tracker", `displayNameHtml` unset; `loginTheme`, `accountTheme` and `emailTheme` = `finance-tracker`, the client `finance-tracker` with no login theme of its own (a second app gets its own theme later); SMTP's `fromDisplayName` = "Finance Tracker" (now `finance-nl.com`), every other SMTP key unchanged: the command writes the whole `smtpServer` map as read back, changing only that key, the password staying `${vault.smtppassword}`; tried on the dev realm first and read back. The rollback values: no `displayName` (D-628 sets `myapps` instead); Keycloak 26.7's default themes, read from the image (expected `keycloak.v2`, `keycloak.v3`, `keycloak`); `fromDisplayName` = `finance-nl.com`. The dev realm gets the same settings: `realm-export.json` for a fresh volume, and a dev-only step in auth_server's README for an existing one.
- D-622 (the PM). Local checks and the deploy's order. The dev stack gets Mailpit, pinned by version and digest with its ports on 127.0.0.1 only, and the dev realm's SMTP points at it; dev only, nothing in `deploy/`. The checklist is finance's "UX-15's deploy checklist with the suite": auth first (push `auth_server`; check `/opt/auth/.deployed-revision`; `sync.sh --dry-run`, then `sync.sh`, which recreates `keycloak` for the mount, sign-in down for about a minute; the smoke test; maintenance access on; kcadm: read the current values printing no secret, set D-621's values, read them back; access off and the `401` check), then finance (`deploy.sh run <M> UX-15`, `e2e:prod`, `finish`, `verify UX-15`, the rollback acceptance, CI on Ubuntu 26.04); rollback blocks for both sides (auth: the realm's old values by kcadm and the sync backup of the files; finance: `3ed8515`, with no condition). No step between 03:30 and 04:30 UTC.
- D-623 (the agent, UX-15). What the pinned image (`quay.io/keycloak/keycloak:26.7.4@sha256:82a77884…`) says, read from its JARs: `keycloak.v2` (login, `org.keycloak.keycloak-themes-26.7.4.jar`) and `keycloak.v3` (account, `keycloak-account-ui-26.7.4.jar`) use PatternFly 5 (`pf-v5-` classes, the vendor folder `patternfly-v5`; the image carries no minor version); the default themes are `keycloak.v2` for login, `keycloak.v3` for account and `keycloak` for email (`ThemeSelectorProvider`'s constants); `RealmBean.getDisplayNameHtml` falls back to `displayName`, so the header shows "Finance Tracker" with `displayNameHtml` unset; and `AccountConsole#getReferrer` turns `referrer` alone into the client's root and base URL, checking a `referrer_uri` against the redirect URIs only when one is sent. So the app sends `referrer` only and no redirect URI is added. Production's client `finance-tracker` was created with the name "Finance Tracker" and base URL `https://app.finance-nl.com/` (auth_server's PRODUCTION.md); the checklist reads both back.
- D-624 (the agent, UX-15). The account console loads the script: `keycloak.v3`'s `index.ftl` renders `scripts` from `theme.properties` as module scripts, so `js/theme.js` sets `data-theme` there too, deferred; until it runs `css/tokens.css` follows the device by CSS (`:root:not([data-theme])`, dark → Blue's tokens), which is also the sign-in pages' state without the script. The masthead's wordmark is drawn by CSS (Keycloak's logo `img` hidden, the brand link's `::after` "Finance Tracker", Plex Sans 600 20px), not an SVG.
- D-625 (the agent, UX-15). `css/tokens.css` (the fonts' faces and the three themes' tokens) is one file, generated once from finance's `frontend/src/index.css` and `fonts.css` and copied unchanged into `login/` and `account/`, and the nine font files and `OFL.txt` are in both types' `resources/fonts/`: a theme's types don't share resources without an `import` of a common theme, which would replace `keycloak.v2`'s own `import=common/keycloak`.
- D-626 (the agent, UX-15). The email layout turns the body's own link, Keycloak's `<a>` in its own paragraph, into the button by rewriting that `<a>`'s attributes in `template.ftl` (a regular expression over the body as the template renders it), and puts the plain link under it; every `<p>` gets a 16px bottom margin. The footer line: "Sent by Finance Tracker's sign-in. If you didn't expect it, you can ignore it." The layout's `meta` says `color-scheme: light only`. Amended in UX-19 (D-819): the plain link under the button is gone, and the footer reads "Finance Tracker · app.finance-nl.com".
- D-627 (the agent, UX-15). Keycloak 26.7 shows no password fields on the registration form while `verifyEmail` is on: the password is asked after the email is verified (`RegistrationPassword.buildPage`, unless the form's config `always_set_password_on_register_form` is set). Production has `verifyEmail` on, so its registration looks so already; the theme changes nothing of it. "New user? Register" and the other info lines (`#kc-info`) stand under the panel by CSS, the panel leaving room for them. A button without a kind of its own (`keycloak.v2` defines no default class, "Account already exists"'s two) is a default button; a link drawn as a button has its label centred.
- D-628 (the owner, UX-15, in place of D-621's "no displayName"). kcadm can't unset a realm's display name on Keycloak 26.7.4: `-d displayName` and an empty value both leave it as it is (tried on throwaway dev realms). Without one the sign-in pages show the realm's name, `myapps`, and the emails `Myapps`. The auth rollback sets `displayName` = `myapps`: the pages read as before, the emails say `myapps`.
- D-629 (the agent, UX-15). The dev realm: `realm-export.json` and the README's dev-only step also give the client `finance-tracker` the name "Finance Tracker" and the base URL `http://localhost:5173/` (the console's "Back to Finance Tracker" needs both, as production's client has them) and `testuser` the role `default-roles-myapps`, which every production user has and the account console needs (without it its API answers 401); the dev SMTP's sender is `noreply@finance-tracker.test`. Mailpit is `axllent/mailpit:v1.32.0` (2026-10-22) by its index digest.
- D-630 (the agent, UX-15). `sync.sh`: a theme change is a file under `themes/` in rsync's own list of files to send (`<f` lines); after the copy every file under `/opt/auth/themes` is set 644 and every directory 755; `keycloak` is restarted after `docker compose up -d` only when its container is the same one as before (recreated, it reads the new theme anyway: the first deploy, which adds the mount, recreates it); the note on sign-in is printed before the question, `--dry-run` says it would restart, `--copy-only` says a restart is needed with the command. As before, `sync.sh` deletes no file on the server.
- D-631 (the agent, UX-15). Keycloak's resource paths (`/resources/<version>/login/finance-tracker/…`) keep their version across a restart (checked on a throwaway 26.7.4 with the cache on), so a browser may keep an older copy of a changed file: a later change gives the changed CSS or script a new file name, named in `theme.properties` (auth_server's PRODUCTION.md, "Theme"). The first deploy is unaffected: every path is new.
- D-632 (the agent, UX-15). The cookie and the link in the app: `cookieDomain` gives no `Domain` for an IPv4 or IPv6 address either; a browser that refuses `document.cookie` leaves the sign-in pages following the device. `accountUrl` is built once from the configured issuer and client id (`app.keycloak.*`, which the backend checks every token's `iss` and `aud` against), the client id URL-encoded; the header shows Account only for an `http:` or `https:` address, and `isMe` doesn't check the field, so an older api or a bad value leaves the item out and nothing else.
- D-633 (the agent, UX-15). The privacy policy (last updated 13 November 2026): the cookie list's introduction says "strictly necessary for the service, or that keep a choice you made", the theme cookie's item says what it holds (only the chosen theme), why (the login pages show it), when it is set (only on a choice), how long (a year from the last visit), where it goes (finance-nl.com's own sites) and that no server reads it, and the consent sentence says "strictly necessary or keeps a choice you made". For the owner to review.
- D-634 (the agent, UX-15). The checks: `10-themes` reads the cookie after each choice (value, domain, `SameSite`, `Secure`) and opens Keycloak's authorization endpoint with `prompt=login` and a fixed PKCE challenge (the client requires S256), its realm from `/api/me`'s `accountUrl`, then checks `html[data-theme]` and the header and leaves the page; `13-account` (A) opens Account from the menu, checks the console's heading, theme and wordmark, and follows "Back to Finance Tracker" to `/`. `npm run screens:keycloak -- <folder>` (local only) takes every page of D-618, the console's three pages and the three emails in throwaway realms `ux15-pages` (set as production's `myapps`, with Google and GitHub shown) and `ux15-idp` (a stand-in provider, hidden, for the first sign-in pages), and deletes both.
- D-635 (the agent, UX-15). `deploy/checks/UX-15.sql` and `UX-15.expected` are UX-14's 12 lines unchanged (no migration, no table, no stored row changed). The checklist runs auth's part before `run` and, since `sync.sh` recreates `keycloak` for the mount, the sign-in pages are down for about a minute then; the finance api isn't touched by it (its users stay signed in until their next refresh, which waits for Keycloak).
- D-636 (the agent, UX-15). The checklist's kcadm reads use `--fields 'displayName,displayNameHtml,loginTheme,accountTheme,emailTheme,smtpServer(*)'`: `smtpServer` without `(*)` prints `{ }` whatever the map holds. The read prints the SMTP map's keys, its login among them and the password as the vault reference `${vault.smtppassword}`, no secret. kcadm's `update` writes the realm back from what it read with the keys given changed, so `-s 'smtpServer.fromDisplayName=Finance Tracker'` writes the whole map as read with that key changed (tried on a dev realm with a map of production's shape: only that key changed, and the rollback gave the map back exactly).
- D-637 (the PM). UX-15 accepted after the PM's review of both commits and of the 156 screenshots in `data/private/ui/UX-15/`. D-623 to D-636 are accepted; the owner confirmed D-633's wording of the privacy policy. A = `87b478bd68380be6640771387b2ea0d23d7ff2af`, M = `56c51bb8b0da1705b9c0624645cc0692025d2252`. The laptop couldn't be reached for the review, so both commits were pushed to branches `review/UX-15` for it; finance's CI ran on that branch (#127, D-640); both branches were deleted after step 9.
- D-638 (the PM). Two outputs of UX-15's checklist differ from its text; neither is a fault, and the checklist stays as committed. A11's last check printed `finance-tracker/css/styles.css` besides the three named files (keycloak.v2's own stylesheet, served through the theme's path) and no header line, its grep pattern being bound to one line (`10-themes` checks the header instead). Step 2 expects the history to end with UX-14's rollback acceptance, but a refused rollback writes no history line: it shows only as its run folder.

- D-640 (the PM). CI on M had three runs of `CI`, all `push`, attempt 1, success, five jobs each: #127 on `review/UX-15`, #128 on `main`, #129 on `feature/family-budget`, so 15 check runs, and `run`'s 1.5 said "15 check runs". `deploy.sh`'s gate counts every run of `ci.yml` for the commit and needs each one to succeed, whatever their number.

- D-649 (the PM). The chart's anatomy, every form. A row per range, month or year (D-652), top to bottom oldest first (D-413's order). Two columns: the labels, `max-content` at most 40% of the chart's width, their text wrapping inside; then the bars. By range the role in Sans 13px `--text` (the period's own role, "Period" or "To date", 600), its range under it in Mono 12px `--muted`; by month `monthLabel`'s month in Mono 13px and `monthNote`'s note under it in 12px `--muted`. In each row two bars, Income above Expenses, from one baseline: a 1px `--chart-grid` line at the start of the bar column, the chart's full height; no value axis and no gridlines. 12px thick by range, 10px by month and by year; 2px between a row's bars; 10px between rows by range, 6px by month and by year; `--chart-income` and `--chart-expense`, square at the baseline, 4px rounded at the end. Each bar's value 6px after its end, Mono 12px `--text`, as the tables format it (`formatMoney`, `rounded` as they do); text never in a series colour. Null: no bar, "—" in `--muted`. MISSING: no bar, "rate missing" as the tables show it. 0 or below: no bar, a 2px `--border` stub at the baseline, and the value, signed. One scale per chart (per currency in each-currency mode): the largest value among its bars and its markers spans the bar column less the room of the widest value text, its characters × 0.6em + 8px, with no measuring (Plex Mono's advance), unless a glyph of a currency's format falls back to another font. The legend above the rows, 12px `--muted`: a 10px swatch with "Income", one with "Expenses", and by month with a comparison the marker with `BEHIND`'s name; "Compare with" above it. The figure keeps its `aria-label` and, in each-currency mode, its `figcaption`; the height follows the rows.
- D-650 (the PM). The comparison by month (D-399's dashed lines) becomes a marker on each bar, at the comparison month's value: a 2px line in `--text`, 4px taller than the bar (2px above, 2px below), with a 1px `--surface` edge each side. None where the comparison's month is null, MISSING, or 0 or below. When the marker lies past its bar's end, the value text follows the marker, 6px after it: a marker never crosses a value. Which comparison is drawn, the toggle and `compare=previous` don't change (`drawable`, `drawnComparison`). `--chart-income-before` and `--chart-expense-before` stay defined (Keycloak's copy, D-625; D-504's guard) though the chart no longer uses them.

Leaving the chart or tapping outside hides it; no `title` attributes. The drawn rows are `aria-hidden`; a visually hidden table carries every row: caption "Income and expenses in <currency>", a row per range, month or year with its income and expenses, and by month with a comparison the comparison's month, its income and its expenses. Amended by D-687 (UX-17): a comparison month with no figure in either series is left out of the panel; a series without a figure shows "—" with no "(—)".

- D-656 (the agent, UX-16). D-649's glyph check, on the `@fontsource/ibm-plex-mono` 400 files the app bundles (latin, latin-ext, cyrillic): every character of the currency formats checked (`€ $ £ ¥ ₽ ₹ ₺ ₴ ₸ ₩ ₪`, digits, `, . - −`, the no-break space U+00A0, letters of "zł", "Kč", "CHF", "A$") has an advance of exactly 0.6em, except the narrow no-break space U+202F, which some locales' formats use as their group separator (French, for one) and which falls back to another font. A narrow no-break space is narrower than 0.6em in any font, so the estimate only over-counts for it and the room is never too small; the chart doesn't measure. The PM may ask for measuring in that case instead.

- D-658 (the agent, UX-16). Placements in the bar column are CSS (`calc((100% - <room>px) * <share>)`), each share a JS number from `toChartNumber` (lengths only). A value's left is its bar's end + 6px, a marker's centre + 7px (its 1px half, then 6px) when the marker lies past the bar, 8px after a stub, 6px from the baseline for "—" and "rate missing"; the room counts every value text the chart shows, "—" and "rate missing" included. A value's box is as tall as its bar (`line-height: 1` in a box of the bar's height), so two values of a row and a marker of the other bar never overlap. A bar above 0 is at least 1px wide. The class names are `ie-*` (`ie-chart`, `ie-legend`, `ie-rows`, `ie-row`, `ie-label`, `ie-bars`, `ie-line`, `ie-bar`, `ie-stub`, `ie-marker`, `ie-value`, `ie-panel`), the form on the chart (`ie-ranges`, `ie-months`, `ie-years`). The rows are one CSS grid with subgrid rows, `fit-content(40%) minmax(0, 1fr)`, the labels `overflow-wrap: anywhere`; the rows touch and their space is the cells' padding, so the baseline runs the chart's full height and a pointed row's background covers it.

- D-684 (the owner, UX-17). Where nothing was posted, the dashboard shows nothing: no empty chart, no column of dashes.
- D-685 (the PM, UX-17). A comparison with nothing posted is left out, in each currency on its own (the one table in the base currency, each currency's table in each currency). "Nothing posted" in a currency: the comparison's own cash flow has no income, expense or exchange-rate line in it (no table for it, or an empty one); a line whose amounts net to 0 is something posted. Such a comparison loses, for that currency: its two columns in "Cash flow by category" (the amount and Change %); its row on the chart by range; its markers and its legend entry on the chart by month; its changes in the headline strip (D-569; already left out where they'd be "—", kept). "Compare with" offers only the comparisons `drawable` allows that at least one currency's table keeps; a `compare=previous` naming one not offered falls back as `drawnComparison` does. A currency whose table doesn't keep the drawn comparison gets no marker and no marker in its legend. The period's own column and row always stay; a category with amounts in a comparison only is still listed with "—" in the period (D-393). A comparison that failed keeps D-401: its error shown, its columns left out. No note in place of a comparison left out.
- D-686 (the PM, UX-17). Empty rows, charts and panels. By month and by year (D-399, D-652), the leading rows with nothing are left out (the period's income and expenses null, and the drawn comparison's too): the chart starts at its first row with a figure. Later rows with "—" stay; MISSING counts as a figure. A chart left without a row isn't drawn: in each currency its figure goes, and the currency's table stays. Daily spending (D-571) isn't shown for a currency none of whose shown days has a figure (nothing posted on any asked day, or no day asked yet); the chart then takes the row's full width at every width. `NoCashFlow`'s text stays for a period and comparisons with nothing in any currency.

- D-688 (the owner, UX-17). The demo goes back at least a year and a month, so that it shows changes over a year.
- D-689 (the PM, UX-17). The demo has 36 months, this month included (`MONTHS` 36). Whatever day it's loaded, it then covers in full the two calendar years before this one, so every period of the Period list compares with a range the demo covers in full. Each monthly entry still falls once in every calendar month the demo covers in full; the opening balances on the 1st of its first month; nothing after today. Every entry kind stays; every entry keeps the ledger's rules; the balances stay plausible on every day and end plausibly. Invented, as before: no real people, companies or brands.
- D-690 (the PM, UX-17). The years differ, plausibly: the salary rises once a year, the rent once a year, the transit pass and a subscription now and then; electricity and heating follow the calendar (higher from November to March); the other monthly amounts vary so that no month repeats the same month a year earlier. The occasional entries recur by year where a person's would: a summer trip each year (somewhere different, partly in dollars), a birthday present, the quarterly interest, freelance projects in different months; the loan to Robin and the split purchase happen once. For every whole month that has its whole month a year earlier in the demo, the personal plan's expenses in euros differ from it by 1% to 35%, and the salary differs. The ledger is the same for the same day and may differ with the day it's loaded on.
- D-691 (the PM, UX-17). The demo family budget (H1) covers the same 36 months from the demo's first day: the monthly shops and water bills, its occasional records by year as D-690 says, and Sam paying back often enough that what Sam owes stays below a few hundred euros on every day.

The backend's months of 0 revaluation, where nothing was held, are no exchange-rate line: otherwise a year earlier before the demo started would keep its column in the base currency. `compareTables` builds each currency's table from the columns it keeps, so the strip's changes (`totalChanges`), the chart by range (`rangeBars`) and the table follow it; `keeps`, `offeredComparisons` (what "Compare with" offers) and `drawnComparison`'s third argument, the offered list.

- D-697 (the agent, UX-17). The demo's monthly entries keep their slots (a fixed 0 to 27 days before the same day of an earlier month), now 36; the account fee moved from day 1 to day 0, so that the last entry stays on the load day (`DemoLedgerView.to`). The yearly entries are on fixed calendar days within the demo's days: freelance projects on the 14th (three a year, in other months each year), pharmacy 9 Feb, the birthday present from family 14 Mar, a bike service 6 Apr, spring shoes 19 Apr, a friend's present 23 May, train tickets 24 Jun, the hotel (dollars) 3 Jul and museum tickets (dollars) 5 Jul, a dental check-up 12 Sep, a jacket 16 Oct and its partial return 27 Oct, holiday presents 15 Dec, the quarterly interest at each quarter's end. The loan to Robin (9 months back), its repayments (7 and 1 months back) and the split purchase (4 months back) are in the last year, whose months have no month a year later, so that each adds to its month's difference from a year earlier rather than taking from the next year's.
- D-698 (the agent, UX-17). D-690's levels are counted back from the load day: the salary €2,760.00 since the last April, €90 or so less for each April before (2,680, 2,590, 2,510); the rent €1,140 since the last September (1,110, 1,080, 1,050); the transit pass, the sports club and the streaming subscription by a table of the years back; every other euro amount from a table by calendar month (energy higher from November to March) at a price level about 4.5 % lower for each calendar year back, plus a step of 0 to 3 % by the year and month. So every euro expense is 1.5 % to 8 % above the same month's a year earlier, and the months' expenses differ from a year earlier by +2.8 % to +8.8 % on 2026-11-13; income moves with the freelance projects and the interest. A ledger loaded a year later has the same current salary and shows the earlier years lower. Amended by D-712 (UX-17-doc): `grown`'s step of 0 to 3 % depends on the year only (with each item's own salt), not on the month, whose term (month × 7) is 0 modulo 7; the amounts, D-690's bounds and the tests are unchanged.
- D-699 (the agent, UX-17). Amounts the demo works out from its balances, in date order: the credit card's bill pays all the card owes (so no refund can turn the card positive; the jacket and its return are on the current account now, which a debit card pays); "Monthly savings" moves what the current account holds above €3,200, rounded down to €50, between €200 and €1,500; the cash withdrawal tops cash up to about €120 (rounded up to €10, €20 to €200); the partner's share of the groceries pays all the shared account holds; the quarterly interest is 0.35 % of the savings; dollars are exchanged on 12 Feb, May, Aug and Nov when more than $1,600 are held, keeping $1,100, rounded down to $100, at €0.862 to €0.921 a dollar by year. The dollar account starts with $1,450, and FAMILY_DEBT with €84.20 the partner owed when the ledger started, so that the first month has "Partner's share of the groceries" like every other (six opening balances). With the demo family's payments and settlements the current account still never goes below zero (`DemoLedgerTests.theFamilysPaymentsLeaveTheAccountsAboveZero`).

Sam pays back every third month what Sam owes in euros, rounded down to €10, and each 20 Sep all Sam owes in dollars, into the dollar account (`DemoFamily.Settlement.currency`, passed to `NewSettlement`). `DemoFamily.owed` works out what Sam owes on a day, for the plan and its tests: between −€150 and €300 on every day of four years.

- D-739 (the PM, DOC-1c). CI #143 attempt 1 (run 43593168410, on `0442d46`, a documents-only commit that passed as CI #142 at the same time) failed in the Frontend job's Test step: `src/UX13.test.tsx`, "the headline strip (D-569) > names the scope under Income and Expenses, as Show and Records do", line 333, `expected [ '/api/settings', …(48) ] to include '/api/reports/daily-cash-flow?from=2026-10-01&to=2026-10-31&scope=FAMILY&familyLedgerId=7&currency=BASE'`. A race in the test, not in the app: the test waited for the cash flow's figures, then asserted at once that the daily spending's request had been made, which starts later. Fixed by waiting for it; every frontend assertion of a request or a late-loaded element is checked for the same race; the frontend suite run at least 5 times and `UX13.test.tsx` at least 20, with no retries added to the test configuration, which would hide races instead of fixing them.
- D-740 (the agent, DOC-1c). The race, as found: in the test's third render (`scope=family`, no `family=`) the first cash flow and daily requests go out without a budget, since the scope's one budget is known only once `/api/family-ledgers` answers; the figure €2,000.00 shows from that first answer (the stub ignores the scope), so the wait resolves early; the render that adds the scope notes also sets the daily path with `familyLedgerId=7`, and its request starts in that render's effects, which React runs after the DOM change that resolves Testing Library's next check. Shown with a throwaway change, never committed: `Dashboard.tsx` asking a later change of the daily path 50 ms after its render (the first path at once): the old line 333 failed 3 runs of 3 with CI's message, the other 22 tests passing; the fixed test passed 3 of 3. The rule applied: an assertion that a request was made is safe when the preceding wait observed that request or its result, or the request was made before the wait (in the first render, which Testing Library's `render` flushes, or in a synchronous handler); a log of `calls` right after each `render` in `UX3`, `UX11` and `UX13` showed every other asserted request there made in the first render. Changed: `UX13.test.tsx` line 333 only. Checked and left, with their reasons in DOC-1c's change log entry: `UX13` 354, 366, 377 and 384 (made in the first render), `UX3` 194 to 197, 205, 206 and 261 (the same), 278 (the alert is the PATCH's answer), `UX11` 100 and 108 (the same render as the request waited for), 264 (the first render), `Family.test.tsx` 129, 198, 250, 269 and 389 (after their own requests' results), `FamilyLifecycle.test.tsx` 88, 89, 155, 223 and 267 (the same; 267 asserts that nothing is asked), and the other files' request assertions (`Import`, `importFilter`, `FamilyPayer`, `DateInput`, `Export`, `UX9`, `StartPages`, `UX8`, `EntryForms`, `invite`, `Invite`: results waited for, or `confirm` and handlers called synchronously). The late-loaded elements read right after a wait in the five named files are rendered by the same answer or the same render as what was waited for.

The first month of a file that starts within it is compared from the file's first row date on both sides: his benefit rows dated from that day, and his shares of the incomes the others received dated from that day; the month keeps its name (`2024-02`). Not the whole month: the file holds no row of the days before its first, so comparing them would give the same false warning as D-757 within one month (an income of the 7th against a file from the 15th: 0.00 against his share). And not from the next whole month: that would drop the file's own rows of its first month from the check. Same days on both sides is D-256's period, which is day-exact. `MemberImportApiTests.aFileStartingWithinAMonthComparesItFromItsFirstDay`: from the 9th, February's income of the 7th and his benefit row of the 7th both drop out, no month and no warning; from the 7th, the day counts and a row changed to 49 still warns against 50.00.

- D-796 (the owner, UX-18). For family records the summary's Amount column goes from the whole record to the user's part: "Spent" (the user's own expenses plus family expense records in full, whoever paid), "Others' shares" (what of those records falls on the other members), then Out as today. Incomes the same way: "Received", "Others' shares", then In as today. Net as today.
- D-797 (the PM, UX-18). A family record counts through the user's share entry (kind FAMILY_SHARE, live family link) that the filter matches, once per record. Its full amount goes to Spent (or Received), in the currency the share entry is posted in, and the full amount minus the user's share goes to Others' shares. This holds whoever paid: a record the partner paid counts the same way. Invariant, per currency: Spent + Others' shares = Out, and Received + Others' shares = In; Out, In and Net are exactly what they are today.
- D-798 (the PM, UX-18). The paying side of a counted record (kind FAMILY_PAYMENT, the rows "Family payment" and "Family income received") isn't shown again: its money is in Spent or Received. A paying side whose record isn't counted (e.g. an account filter that matches the payment but not the share) stays under Transfers, as today. Settlement sides, family opening balances and corrections, and ordinary transfers, exchanges and loans stay under Transfers.
- D-799 (the PM, UX-18). Signs and order: "Spent" with −, "Others' shares" with +, "Out" with −; "Received" with +, "Others' shares" with −, "In" with +; Net; Transfers without a sign. Signs because in this UI an amount without one is a transfer. Spent, Received and their Others' shares lines appear only when that side's Others' shares isn't 0; otherwise the row is as today. Each line only when it isn't 0; per currency, never added across currencies. Under 40rem they are labelled lines like the others, each side's three lines together.
- D-800 (the PM, UX-18). D-524 said the summary repeats the rows' cells. For counted family records it no longer does: a purchase has two rows (payment and share) but counts once, by the record's full amount. Everything else still matches the cells. The e2e comparison follows this rule.
- D-801 (the PM, UX-18). UX-18 has a deploy (api and web; no migration, Flyway stays at V17). It also takes DOC-1d's landing page text to production. Its checklist goes in `deploy/RUNBOOK.md`, as UX-17's did, from `0c1bfb6`.
- D-802 (the agent, UX-18). With the family switch off nothing is counted and the summary is as before UX-18, as `recordPayee` is (the same `:rpOn` parameter): every family row is then an ordinary row of the user's ledger.
- D-803 (the agent, UX-18). A side is the share entry's own sign, the rows' rule: an expense's share (shown with −) counts on the Spent side, an income's (+) on the Received side, and so does a refund's share, which comes in (+): Received its full amount, Others' shares the rest. A deleted record is never counted (its entries are gone).
- D-804 (the agent, UX-18). Others' shares are the record's full amount less the share entry's own amount in the record's currency (what Out or In already holds for it), not `family_share` read again, so that D-797's invariant holds by construction. A record whose share of the user's is 0 has no share entry and isn't counted: its payment stays under Transfers (D-798's rule for a record that isn't counted). A currency whose only figures were counted payments (a payment in another currency through FX_EXCHANGE) leaves the list, as a currency with nothing in it never was in it.
- D-805 (the agent, UX-18). The new fields of `EntrySummary.CurrencyTotals`: `spent` (`outgoing` + `spentOthersShares`), `spentOthersShares`, `received` (`incoming` + `receivedOthersShares`) and `receivedOthersShares`, always present, each at or above 0; `incoming`, `outgoing` and `transfers` keep their names, `transfers` less the counted paying sides.
- D-806 (the agent, UX-18). The order D-799 leaves open between the sides is today's: the incoming side (Received, Others' shares, In), then the outgoing side (Spent, Others' shares, Out), then Net and Transfers. "Others’ shares" is written with the typographic apostrophe, as the app's "Others’ records"; Received is coloured as In and Spent as Out.

- D-822 (the agent, UX-19). The cause of D-639's item 3: PatternFly 5's light-scope components (`.pf-v5-c-data-list`, `-form-control`, `-alert`, `-menu`, `-select`, `-table`, `.pf-v5-t-light`) set `--pf-v5-global--Color--100` and `--Color--200`, the links, the borders and the primary colour to their `--dark-*` values, and its dark-scope ones (`.pf-v5-c-login__header`, `__footer`, `.pf-v5-t-dark` and others) to their `--light-*` values. UX-15's `login.css` mapped only the top-level names, so inside those scopes PatternFly's own greys came back: "Select login method"'s descriptions (`pf-v5-u-color-200` in a data list) computed to `#6A6E73` in all three themes, not only Blue and Dark (in Light it is close to `--muted`, `#566173`). The fix maps the scoped names behind them to the tokens: `Color--dark-100`, `--dark-200`, `--light-100`, `--light-200` to `--text` and `--muted`, the links' `--dark`, `--dark--hover`, `--light`, `--light--hover` and `--primary-color--dark-100` and `--light-100` to `--accent`. Measured on the dev stack: the descriptions are `--muted` in each theme after. The other PatternFly colour variables of the same kind are left unmapped and listed here; whether a page shows them differently from the theme wasn't measured, and the 156 screenshots before and after differ on "Select login method" only (apart from the OTP pages' random secrets), so the mapping changed nothing else: `--pf-v5-global--BorderColor--dark-100` and `--light-100` and `--BackgroundColor--light-100` and `--dark-100` `--icon--Color--light` and `--dark`, and `--disabled-color--100` and `--200`.

The emails need no rename: Keycloak renders them on the server.

A third commit on top of `2b8e620`, same stage, before the deploy: reproduce, fix the test, check the other helpers that sort lines holding ids as text; no production code changed.
- D-833 (the PM, UX-19's third commit). No test's expected order depends on the ids' digits: where a helper sorts lines holding ids as text and the order isn't what the test asserts, the test compares in any order, or the expected lines are sorted by the same rule.
- D-834 (the agent, UX-19's third commit). The cause, confirmed: `linked()` sorts "link:type:<category id> <name>" as text, and the expected list put Groceries before Rent, true only while both ids have the same number of digits; Groceries' family category is made with the family budget and Rent 2 ids later, so the order flips exactly when Rent's id reaches a power of ten and Groceries' doesn't. Reproduced locally by setting `app.category`'s id sequence so that Groceries was 999998 and Rent 1000000 (10^6 rather than 1000, since the shared test database's ids may already be past 1000): the same four swaps. It is steady on #155 and absent on #154 because CI seeds the classes' random order with `github.run_number` (D-99), which a re-run keeps: the same seed, the same class order on a fresh database, so the same sequence values on every attempt; #154's seed put the two ids on the same side of a power of ten.
- D-835 (the agent, UX-19's third commit). The api's image changes with a test-only commit: `backend/Dockerfile` copies `src/`, tests included, into the build stage, so its cache misses and `mvn package` writes a new jar, and the pom sets no `project.build.outputTimestamp`. Built on the laptop at `2b8e620` and at the third commit: image IDs `b8c4f3c6…` and `7d4bec90…`, jar SHA-256 `ca177357…` and `84432d8e…`, and every entry of the two jars byte for byte the same (only the zip's timestamps differ). So UX-19's checklist expects both images new, the api recreated and sessions ended. A reproducible jar (an `outputTimestamp`), or a Dockerfile that copies only `src/main`, goes to "Later".
- D-836 (the agent, UX-19's third commit). The helpers, each read: `FamilyEntryCategoryApiTests.linked` (line 35) affected, in `paymentsReceiptsAndSharesNameTheRecordsCategory` (two ids behind the same prefix): now `containsExactlyInAnyOrder`, and its comment no longer says "then entry id"; its other uses are `contains` or one line. `FamilyEntryCategoryApiTests.links` (line 45): not affected, its lines are link types without ids. `FamilyRecordPayeeApiTests.linked` (line 55): not affected, every exact comparison holds one record id, its lines told apart by their link type before it (`PAYMENT` before `SHARE`); the others filter or use `contains`. `FamilyCashFlowScopeApiTests.totals` (line 239): no ids, "CURRENCY total" with fixed amounts, so its text order is the same on every run. `ApiTests` (line 275): HTTP method names. The only other `sorted()` over strings, `DataIsolationApiTests` line 2145, sorts request-mapping parameter expressions, no ids.
- D-837 (the agent, UX-19's third commit). The reproduction is not kept as a test: it needs the shared database's category sequence set to just below a power of ten, which moves every later class's ids (to about 10^6) and would make other classes' ids depend on whether it ran; it was a temporary override of `createTheFamily`, removed before the commit.

- D-851 (the owner, FX-1). RUB per EUR from 2022-03-02 on comes automatically from the Bank of Russia. It amends D-49's "RUB after 2022-03-01 is entered by hand"; manual rates stay available and unchanged.
- D-852 (the PM, FX-1). Only RUB is loaded from the CBR: its official EUR rate (currency code R01239), which already is RUB for one euro, as `exchange_rate` stores every rate. `Value / Nominal`, the decimal comma read as a point. A record with an unexpected currency id, a nominal that isn't positive or a value that isn't a positive number makes the whole load fail, and nothing is written. The response is windows-1251.
- D-853 (the PM, FX-1). CBR rates are shared by all users like the ECB's: `source = 'CBR'`, `user_id` NULL. V18 allows `'CBR'` in `exchange_rate_source_check` and puts `source` into the unique key, so the two shared sources can never overwrite each other; every `ON CONFLICT` target names the new key. The ECB loader's "latest shared day" reads ECB rows only (`latestEcbDate`), since CBR rows dated tomorrow would break its gap logic; every query on `exchange_rate` is checked for an assumption that a shared rate is an ECB rate.
- D-854 (the PM, FX-1). `RateBook`: of the rates that apply on a day (an ECB rate at most 7 days old, a CBR rate at most 15 days old, the user's latest manual rate at any age), the one with the latest date wins; on the same date, manual over ECB over CBR. D-91 stays true as a special case. Everything else in D-49, D-90 and D-91 is unchanged: nothing guessed, no later rate used, a manual rate older than 31 days marked stale.
- D-855 (the PM, FX-1). `CBR_DAYS = 15`: the CBR's longest pause between rates is 13 days, over the January holidays. A CBR rate older than that is never used, as with `ECB_DAYS`.
- D-856 (the PM, FX-1). Loading: `CbrRateLoader`, `CbrClient` and `CbrSchedule`, mirroring the ECB's (`app.rates.cbr.enabled`, `app.rates.cbr.url`, `app.rates.cbr.cron`). At startup, in the background, and at 13:00 and 17:00 Europe/Moscow every day. The first load (no CBR rows) asks for 2022-03-02 to tomorrow; later loads from the latest CBR date stored minus 10 days to tomorrow. It upserts in one transaction. A failed load is logged and the next one catches up. The log line gives the count and the first and last dates.
- D-857 (the PM, FX-1). The UI calls the source "CBR": the Rates page's Source column, and `rateSourceText` gives "CBR rate of …". The texts that said "else the ECB's" name both sources. The Rates page's "not used: more than … days old" gives each source's limit.
- D-858 (the PM, FX-1). Once only, in the same transaction as the first CBR load (the one that finds no CBR rows), every MANUAL rate of any user whose quote is RUB and for which a CBR rate exists with the same date and a numerically equal rate is deleted (the owner's CSV of the CBR's rates, 1168 rows, 2022-03-02 to 2026-11-14). Manual rates that differ, or that have no CBR rate on their date, stay. It logs how many were deleted and how many manual RUB rates remain, without user ids. Later loads never delete anything.
- D-859 (the agent, FX-1). The rollback below V18 has a condition, kept in the checklist and not in `rollback.sh`: `d9cf51f`'s code can't run on V18, since its upserts (the ECB loader's, a manual rate's) name the old key, which no longer exists, and `RateSource.valueOf` throws on a `CBR` row. So the rollback's database step, as `finance` in one transaction, deletes the CBR rows, puts V17's `exchange_rate_key` and `exchange_rate_source_check` back and deletes V18's row of Flyway's history (so that a later deploy applies V18 again; Flyway ignores an applied migration it doesn't know, but would never re-run one it has recorded), then `rollback.sh`. The manual RUB rates D-858 deleted aren't restored (the owner uploads the CSV again, or the PM restores a dump). A V18 condition in `rollback.sh` itself is left for the PM ("Later").
- D-860 (the agent, FX-1). `ExchangeRateRepository.find` takes the source, since two shared rates of one day and currency may now exist; `findLatest` (the Rates page's latest rate when none applies) orders the same date as `RateBook` does: manual, ECB, CBR. `findForPeriod` already took the latest per currency and source.
- D-861 (the agent, FX-1). "Tomorrow" is `Today.utc()` plus a day (D-117, no implicit clock), not Moscow's; between 21:00 and 24:00 UTC Moscow is already a day ahead, and the next load fetches what that one didn't.
- D-862 (the agent, FX-1). `Value / Nominal` is divided to 8 decimals HALF_UP (`exchange_rate.rate` is NUMERIC(19, 8); the CBR's values have 4, so a nominal of 1 is exact). An answer must be `ValCurs`, a date may appear once, and an answer without records (a window of holidays) writes nothing and isn't an error.
- D-863 (the agent, FX-1). The CBR's loader is off in every test context, the `import` profile and the local stack (`CBR_RATES_ENABLED: "false"` in the root `docker-compose.yml`, while the ECB's stays on there: no local run calls cbr.ru), and in step 12's rehearsal command.
- D-864 (the agent, FX-1). `04-family-f8` no longer expects RUB to be missing: it reads `/api/rates` and, where the CBR's RUB rate applies (production), expects A's and B's totals with "RUB, CBR rate of …" before A's own rate; locally, with no CBR rows, "No RUB rate" as before. It opens `/rates` directly instead of the "enter a rate" link, which only the missing case shows. No spec shows "CBR rate of …" locally: the local stack loads no CBR rate and the specs write only through the app; the wording is covered by Vitest (`dashboard.test.ts`, `currency.test.ts`, `FamilyCurrencies.test.tsx`) and the precedence by `RateBookTests`.

Targets on the restored dump of 2026-11-15, one warm-up then the median of 3: each request of D-894's scenarios at most 1,000 ms; each request of the default view (Entries with no query: the month of the latest entry) at most 300 ms; and the wall time of the page's requests sent together for All time + Expenses + one category reported. - D-902 (the agent, ENT-3b). - D-908 (the agent, REH-1). The script's rule: a family budget is a candidate when it has an ACTIVE owner with an account and another ACTIVE member with an account; the partner's seat that step 12 imports is a member with an account once claimed (D3c), a demo household's partner is a seat without one, so it is no candidate. With exactly one candidate the script maps its two members as before; with none or several it stops (exit 3), naming each candidate by id and creation time and asking for `--family <id>`; `--family` takes a candidate only (else the same message).
