Risks: the api is unavailable for the seconds the database is away and may need a restart; a pin that changes the major version must never pass the guard; the restore test and the dumps are the last resort. Until then the pin stays 17.11, which has no known issue that this app's use reaches.
- D-940 (the agent, OPS-5; the PM's item 3, D-848). The server's build cache is bounded by the deploy script, not by the daemon. **What the code says** (the measurement of D-944 confirms or rejects it): `deploy.sh`'s step 3.3 builds both images on the server; step 3.5's `docker image prune -f` removes dangling images only, never build cache; the cache (13.63 GB, 12.83 GB reclaimable on 2026-11-16; images 2.8 GB) grows with every deploy's layers and the Maven cache mount (`RUN --mount=type=cache,target=/root/.m2` in `backend/Dockerfile`), and `prune_commit_tags` keeps images to the last three revisions. **The bound:** new step 3.7, after 3.6's pages passed, best effort: `timeout 300 docker builder prune -f --filter until=72h --reserved-space 3GB` (flag names checked in `docker builder prune --help` of Docker 29.8.1 on the laptop; the checklist prints the server's 29.10.0's help first): what a build used within 72 hours stays, and 3 GB of the rest is kept (BuildKit's reserved space). A failure or a timeout warns (`WARNING: the build cache wasn't pruned`) and never fails the deploy, which is over by then; a failed deploy never reaches it. It prints `df -h /` before and after and the prune's last line, and the summary has `Build cache:`. The step takes effect from the next deploy (a run executes the script the clone had before its merge, like `numbers.sql`); so `deploy.sh prune-cache` runs the same bound by hand under the lock (exit 1 if the prune failed), and OPS-5's checklist runs it once after `finish`. Never run on the laptop (CLAUDE.md): the stub tests cover the calls, the real flags' effect is first seen in the checklist's output. **The server's rule (the reason is that both stacks' Postgres volumes live there):** on the server only the deploy scripts' own `docker image prune -f` (3.5) and `docker builder prune -f --filter until=72h --reserved-space 3GB` (3.7, `prune-cache`) run; never `docker system prune`, `volume prune`, `container prune`, `network prune`, `-a`, `--volumes`, or any other filter, by hand or in a checklist. The laptop's rule stays. **The alternative, BuildKit's GC in `/etc/docker/daemon.json`:** plus, it needs no deploy and bounds the cache whatever builds it; minus, no `daemon.json` exists and the runbook's step says STOP on one (it may set another log driver, D-281), the setting is outside the repository and its tests, a restart of the daemon restarts both stacks' containers (the auth server's sign-in with them), and a `daemon.json` mistake stops Docker. The two can be combined later; B is right whatever the measurement shows.

The rollback of A is the old compose file put back and `docker compose up -d`; of B, `rollback.sh 7fae522` (images only; the same code and schema).
- D-944 (the agent, OPS-5; D-848). What the checklist's read-only pre-flight measures and what each answer decides: `df -h /`, `du -x` of `/var/lib/docker` and `/var/lib/containerd` (where the images and BuildKit's cache live in the containerd store) say where the space is; `docker system df` the build cache's share against the images'; `docker buildx du | tail -n 4` its total and reclaimable; `docker buildx du --filter type=exec.cachemount` the Maven cache (one record, expected hundreds of MB, not growing per deploy); the count of records older than 72 h, 168 h and 336 h what the bound would free. **Hypothesis confirmed** if the cache is the largest consumer, most of it is older than 72 h and the Maven cache is small beside it (layers of past builds fill it); then 3.7 frees most of the 12.83 GB at the first run and keeps the cache near 3 GB plus the last 72 hours' builds. **Rejected** if something else is larger (the journal, `/var/backups/pg`, `/root/auth-sync-backups`, the images): the PM decides what next, 3.7 stays as a bound. Hetzner's backup sizes are no measure of freed space (they may not shrink); `df` is. The numbers go into the next batch's first commit.

It reconnects by itself, as rehearsed on the laptop (the real api with a pool of 5, a production dump of 2026-11-16, Postgres 17.11 to 17.12 and back on the same named volume, a request every 100 to 250 ms): one or two requests answered 500 and `/actuator/health` 503 for about 0.3 s, then 200 again, no request over 1 s; the log has one "Failed to validate connection … terminating connection due to administrator command" and nothing else. - D-970 (the PM, PERF-1). In this order, stopping at the first that meets every target: (1) the query only: a true estimate of the payees' set (drop join conditions that a constraint implies, naming the constraint, never one that only today's data makes true; `RULE_FROM` and `RULE_WHERE` stay one rule for `join` and `joinSet`, D-900); (2) keep `noNestedLoops` only for the statements that still need it, each named; (3) only then one migration, V19, kept only with a measured gain. If nothing meets the targets, the workaround stays as it is.
- D-971 (the PM, PERF-1). PERF-1 is the first stage of the next batch; a V19 would be the batch's one migration (D-879). No deploy checklist in this stage.
- D-972 (the agent, PERF-1; D-970's hypothesis checked). The planner's estimate of the payees' set is the cause, and the chain of joins is where it goes wrong, but dropping conditions doesn't repair it. On the dump of 2026-11-17 (the owner's 23,997 entries, `testuser2`; the script's mapping put the other participant, 19,027 entries, on `testuser`) the set is estimated at 28 rows and holds 19,464 (without any switch, after the change of D-973, at 12 against 19,464): the redundant `payer.id = pl.member_id` filter that the planner adds beside an index condition divides the estimate by 8, `payers.ledger_id = pe.ledger_id` by 12, and the others by 2 to 7 each; no formulation tried (`pl.member_id = rr.payer_member_id`, fewer conditions) removes them, since they come from the equivalence classes, and an index changes no estimate. With 12 rows the planner takes nested loops over index scans (12,000 probes, 128 to 136 ms for the set alone, 69 ms as hash joins). Extended statistics in Postgres 17 cover the columns of one table and no join, so no V19 helps the estimate itself.
- D-973 (the agent, PERF-1; D-970 step 1). The rule drops four join conditions that composite foreign keys imply (named in `RecordPayees.RULE_FROM`'s comment, each holds for any data the database accepts): `rr.ledger_id = rl.family_ledger_id` and `pl.family_ledger_id = rr.ledger_id` (`family_entry_link_family_ledger_id_record_id_fkey`), `payer.ledger_id = rr.ledger_id` (`family_record_ledger_id_payer_member_id_fkey`) and `pc.ledger_id = pe.ledger_id` (`journal_entry_ledger_id_payee_id_fkey`); the first two columns of each are NOT NULL or the join on the other column drops the row. It doesn't give the planner a true estimate (D-972) but removes a nested-loop plan's extra probes: without any switch the default view's payee breakdown went from 538 to 71 ms. With D-974's rewrite as well it still doesn't meet D-969 without the switch (D-975).
- D-974 (the agent, PERF-1). The summary's own statements read the postings fewer times, the same answers: the counts read them once into `pl` and `ent` (one row per entry: has a counterparty, a category, an account other than FX_EXCHANGE) and take "without a payee" and "without a category" as the count of entries less the count of distinct entries that have one, in place of an anti-join per row; the currencies statement works out `direction` and the positive lines from one pass (`EntryClassification.ctes(…, lines = true)`; for the statements that don't also add up the lines it measured slower, so they keep the direct form). On the dump: the counts 332 to 257 ms (psql, hash joins) and the currencies 173 to 142.

Measured on the dump (ms, the median of 3; All time / + Expenses / + Expenses and one category / default view): the summary without any switch 540 / 638 / 838 / 95 against 462 / 596 / 477 / 186 with it on everything (the old placement), so the counts need it for D-969's 500 ms and for the third column; a list filtered by payee or text took 1,120 to 1,171 ms with the switch and 233 to 309 without; the payee breakdown 353 / 376 / 326 / 181 with it, 392 / 456 / 411 / 71 without. The headers are `finance.caddy`'s, copied (a snippet is not shared between two imported files safely), and `deploy/check-site.sh` now validates both files together, runs `caddy fmt` on both and prints a warning when the four security directives differ (tried: a changed CSP in the apex file gave the warning). - D-984 (the agent, WEB-1). The host test is `isApexHost(hostname)` in `links.ts`, the one place: exactly two labels (`finance-nl.com`), so `app.finance-nl.com`, `localhost` (the dev stack, `e2e:local`) and an IP address are not the apex and start as before; the app's origin from the apex is `appOrigin` (`app.` + the apex). The branch is `startApex(place, cookies)` in `apex.tsx`, called first by `main.tsx` after `startTheme()`, with `window.location` and `document.cookie`, and returning null for any other host. A test reaches the apex branch by giving it `{ hostname: 'finance-nl.com', replace }` and a cookie string; the dev stack cannot (no way to put localhost on a two-label host without a hosts entry and a Vite `allowedHosts` change, not done), so the real thing is the production spec (D-982), which is skipped until the DNS is live.
- D-985 (the agent, WEB-1). Where the hint is set and removed: `main.tsx` sets it right after a valid `/api/me` and clears it on a 401 or a 403 of that call; `api.ts` clears it on any 401 or 403 (a session that ends while the app is open); `logOut` clears it before the form posts. A backend that is down or answers something else changes nothing. A stale hint (the session expired while the browser stayed closed) sends the visitor to the app, which sends them to the login: harmless, and it is the same page a bookmark would show. The cookie is written only by the app's host (`Domain=finance-nl.com`), which the apex can read; the apex writes none.
- D-986 (the agent, WEB-1). The apex's static list is `/`, `/privacy`, `/privacy.html`, `/theme.js`, `/favicon.svg`, `/favicon.ico` and `/assets/*`, in `finance-apex.caddy`'s `@landing`; any other file under `frontend/public/` that the landing page later loads must be added there, or the apex answers it with a 308 to the app. `/index.html` is a 308 too. A POST (the logout form) to the apex follows the 308 with its method.
- D-987 (the agent, WEB-1). Not changed, noted: the theme is kept per origin in `localStorage`, so a visitor who chose a theme at the app sees the device's on the apex's landing page (the cookie `ft_theme` is readable there, but `theme.js` reads only the storage; D-497); a privacy policy served at both hosts is the same file. Adding an A record for the apex does not touch the MX and TXT records of finance-nl.com (the sender `noreply@` and `serge@`); the checklist's DNS step prints every record of the apex and stops if one is not as expected.
- D-988 (the agent, WEB-1). The batch's stage name for `deploy.sh run` is `WEB-1`; `deploy/checks/WEB-1.sql` and `.expected` are OPS-6b's 18 lines (no migration, no table added, `numbers.sql` unchanged), tried on the dev stack's database, which Flyway migrated to V18 (`docker exec … psql` as `finance`): the output equals `WEB-1.expected`. A database restored from a dump renders constraints differently, so none is printed (D-917).
- D-989 (HIST-0, ADR 0006). HIST-0 is design only (the PM).
- D-990 (HIST-0, ADR 0006). One model for the action log and record history: every change to a record makes a new immutable version (the PM).
- D-991 (HIST-0, ADR 0006). A snapshot is a marker of a moment in the history, not a copy; the state then is derived from versions (the PM).
- D-992 (HIST-0, ADR 0006). In a family budget a change by one member reaches the others only when they accept it; only "accept" for now (Sergey).
- D-993 (HIST-0, ADR 0006). Restoring a snapshot gives "archived" status to every record changed since and makes the versions as of the snapshot current again; nothing is lost; no retention or cleanup yet (Sergey).
- D-994 (HIST-0, ADR 0006). Versions, snapshots and restore cover personal ledgers and family budgets from the start, with notifications for family members (Sergey).
- D-995 (HIST-0, ADR 0006). No new version when every field is unchanged; any change makes one; the Excel import follows the same rule (Sergey).
- D-996 (HIST-0, ADR 0006). "Delete all my data" removes the user's history too; the privacy policy states what is kept and for how long (Sergey).
- D-997 (HIST-0, ADR 0006). Notifications are in-app only (the PM).
- D-998 (the agent, HIST-0, ADR 0006). A record is the user-visible unit (section 2.1); derived family rows are not records.
- D-999 (the agent, HIST-0, ADR 0006). Settings, manual rates, memberships, invites and ledger settings are log events with old and new values, not versions.
- D-1000 (the agent, HIST-0, ADR 0006). Option (b): application-written versions in one JSONB table; current state stays in the live tables.
- D-1001 (the agent, HIST-0, ADR 0006). Canonical content, ids not names, bookkeeping excluded; hash equal means no write at all.
- D-1002 (the agent, HIST-0, ADR 0006). Delete and archive remove the live row; the live tables hold only live records; hot paths gain no predicate.
- D-1003 (the agent, HIST-0, ADR 0006). Revisions are per ledger and per change set; the log of record changes is read from versions; other events go to `activity_event`.
- D-1004 (the agent, HIST-0, ADR 0006). Database backstop (a deferred trigger that wants a version in the same transaction) plus an integrity check and test oracle.
- D-1005 (the agent, HIST-0, ADR 0006). `family_record_change`: dual write in HIST-1, versions are the source from HIST-2, table frozen and dropped later.
- D-1006 (the agent, HIST-0, ADR 0006). `import_batch`, `imported_entry_deleted` and the sync stamps stay; stamps are not content.
- D-1007 (the agent, HIST-0, ADR 0006). Sign-in, sign-out and expiry events by sub only, no email, IP or browser; restarts end sessions silently and the log says so.
- D-1008 (the agent, HIST-0, ADR 0006). A snapshot is `(ledger, revision, name, kind)`; state is derived from versions.
- D-1009 (the agent, HIST-0, ADR 0006). Restore semantics (section 6.3): all or nothing, preview first, records created after become archived, deleted ones return, memberships and derived rows untouched.
- D-1010 (the agent, HIST-0, ADR 0006). Automatic snapshots before every import commit and every restore.
- D-1011 (the agent, HIST-0, ADR 0006). Undoing a restore is restoring its `BEFORE_RESTORE` snapshot.
- D-1012 (the agent, HIST-0, ADR 0006). Family acceptance as catch-up: per-member `accepted_revision`, accept advances to the head; the author is accepted automatically when caught up; a member with pending changes may not change family records or commit an import.
- D-1013 (the agent, HIST-0, ADR 0006). D-10 becomes "debt account equals the family balance as of the accepted revision"; integrity shows pending, not a failure.
- D-1014 (the agent, HIST-0, ADR 0006). The Family tab shows the latest family state with pending marks; labels (category and member names) are live; only record changes are revisions. Label-only changes (a family category rename, and the other changes of D-1014 that change no record) take a ledger revision and make nothing pending: a member's accepted revision advances over them only when he was caught up (D-1159).
- D-1015 (the agent, HIST-0, ADR 0006). Only owners restore a family snapshot; any ACTIVE member creates one.
- D-1016 (the agent, HIST-0, ADR 0006). An import's dry run works with pending changes and says so; its commit is refused until the importer accepts.
- D-1017 (the agent, HIST-0, ADR 0006). Notifications are references (range of revisions, author), no content, coalesced while unread, deleted with the user.
- D-1018 (the agent, HIST-0, ADR 0006). Erasure: delete-all deletes the personal history, snapshots, notifications and events by sub; family versions stay without names and without the leaver's comments, erased by a window query inside `release_family_memberships`.
- D-1019 (the agent, HIST-0, ADR 0006). No automatic deletion of history yet (D-993); sign-in events need a limit before release (proposal: 13 months).
- D-1020 (the agent, HIST-0, ADR 0006). Baseline by an application job plus a lazy baseline on first change, no SQL backfill.
- D-1021 (the agent, HIST-0, ADR 0006). Actor is a `ledger_member` id (personal ledgers have one) with kind USER, SYSTEM or IMPORT.
- D-1022 (the agent, HIST-0, ADR 0006). Family versions hold the family fields only, never the payer's private side (D-16).
- D-1023 (the agent, HIST-0, ADR 0006). Acceptance has its own switch, off by default, so HIST-3 deploys dark.
- D-1024 (the agent, HIST-0, ADR 0006). All migrations additive; rollback is the previous image (corrected by D-1040: the previous image must write normally against the new schema, so the backstop only checks version-aware connections).
- D-1025 (the agent, HIST-0, ADR 0006). The privacy policy section ships with HIST-1, before it collects anything.
- D-1026 (Sergey, HIST-0, ADR 0006). Accept all pending changes at once (catch-up); no per-change accept.
- D-1027 (Sergey, HIST-0, ADR 0006). A member with pending changes accepts first before changing family records or committing an import; the refusal offers "Accept" in the same place.
- D-1028 (Sergey, HIST-0, ADR 0006). The Family tab shows the latest family state with "pending for you" marks.
- D-1029 (Sergey, HIST-0, ADR 0006). While a change is pending, Entries shows live labels with a mark; "others' shares" may be off and the mark says so; HIST-3b only if the marks get in the way.
- D-1030 (Sergey, HIST-0, ADR 0006). Only owners restore a family snapshot; archiving other members' records without a decline is accepted for now.
- D-1031 (Sergey, HIST-0, ADR 0006). A personal snapshot does not undo what the family posted into the member's books.
- D-1032 (Sergey, HIST-0, ADR 0006). Settings and manual rates are log-only, outside snapshots.
- D-1033 (Sergey, HIST-0, ADR 0006). Sign-in events are kept 13 months; no IP address, no browser.
- D-1034 (Sergey, HIST-0, ADR 0006). Notifications also for a member joining, leaving or claiming a seat; unread ones from the same author in the same ledger are merged.
- D-1035 (Sergey, HIST-0, ADR 0006). D-993 as read in section 6.3.
- D-1036 (Sergey, HIST-0, ADR 0006). Automatic snapshots before every import commit and every restore.
- D-1037 (Sergey, HIST-0, ADR 0006). HIST-1 is invisible apart from the privacy policy text; history views come with HIST-2.
- D-1038 (Sergey, HIST-0, ADR 0006). Imports after a restore: Excel stays the source; a record a restore archives is not an in-app deletion, a record it brings back leaves `imported_entry_deleted` (section 6.3).
- D-1039 (the PM, HIST-0, ADR 0006). HIST-1 is the only feature stage of its batch.
- D-1040 (the PM, HIST-0, ADR 0006). Rollback must work after V19 (section 5.5, section 9).
- D-1041 (the PM, HIST-0, ADR 0006). HIST-1's acceptance numbers, on the rehearsal database, median of 3: the Entries timings within PERF-1's targets and plans, an import commit at most 20 % slower, a second import of the same file writing 0 versions and 0 revisions, the baseline job's rows, duration and idempotence, the integrity check passing after the baseline and the random tests.
- D-1042 (the PM, HIST-0, ADR 0006). Sign-in events older than 13 months are deleted by a daily job in HIST-1; versions are never deleted automatically.
- D-1043 (the agent, HIST-1). Versions are written after the write, from the live record: `HistoryService.changed(ledger, type, id)` reads the record through `RecordContents` and writes the next version when the canonical hash differs from the latest one's. Repository saves of an entry, account, category or payee are covered in one place by Spring Data after-save callbacks (`HistoryCallbacks`); deletes, the raw SQL sites (the starter ledger, the demo, the category merge, a member's departure) and `FamilyRecordService` call the service where they write. Reason: one comparison per record whatever the path, nothing to keep in step with each service's own model, and the same loader makes the test oracle's copy.
- D-1044 (the agent, HIST-1; the way it is read changed by D-1061). The state before a change is read from the committed database through a pool of its own (the outer transaction hasn't committed, so that is the state before it), and written first when no version of it exists (`BASELINE`, the lazy baseline of D-1020) or when the latest version no longer equals it (`RECONCILE`: only a write by the previous image can do that, D-1040, or a row that wasn't a record until now, such as a debt account when its member left). The baseline takes the revision before the change's. Reading the committed state costs one more connection for the first change of an existing record in a transaction (not for a record the write inserted, D-1060).
- D-1045 (the agent, HIST-1, D-1040 and D-1004). The backstop knows a version-aware writer by a session setting: every connection of the application's pool runs `SET app.history = 'on'` (`spring.datasource.hikari.connection-init-sql`), and the triggers of V19 do nothing for a connection that hasn't. Trade-off: the previous image, `psql` and the runbook's SQL are unchecked, as before V19 (rollback works, D-1040), and a future writer of the application that is given a connection without the setting would be unchecked too (the Hikari setting is the one place; `HistoryApiTests` fails a raw insert through the pool). A Flyway migration runs on the same pool, so a future migration that changes records must either write versions or set `app.history` to `off` for its transaction.
- D-1046 (the agent, HIST-1). The backstop's marks are written at the write, where the writer is known: row triggers on `journal_entry`, `account`, `category`, `counterparty` and `family_record` insert a mark into the unlogged table `history_pending` (empty at rest) unless `app.writer` is `family-posting` or `delete-all`, and a deferred constraint trigger on that table requires the version at commit and removes the mark. A trigger checking at commit alone could not know the writer, which the posting service resets after each statement. Derived rows are exempt by what they are: entries of a `FAMILY_*` kind, accounts with `family_ledger_id` and the placeholder. A posting or a share alone is not marked: every save of an entry or record changes its row's `version` too, which is what the triggers compare.
- D-1047 (the agent, HIST-1). D-995 for an entry: `EntryService.update` compares the canonical content of the new entry with the live one and, if equal, writes nothing at all (no version, no revision, and the entry's own optimistic-lock version stays), so that the backstop is never asked for a version of a save that changed nothing; the import's `import_version` follows the unchanged `version`, which the sync wanted.
- D-1048 (the agent, HIST-1). The revision is taken lazily, at the first version a transaction writes for a ledger, by `UPDATE ledger SET revision = revision + 1 … RETURNING`, whose row lock lasts until commit, so revisions follow commit order and a transaction that writes no version takes none (a re-import of the same file takes 0). The family code locked the ledger `FOR SHARE` in `FamilyRecordService.lockLedger`: two records' transactions that each held it and then updated the row would deadlock, so it is `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` now, which serialises the record writes of one family, each a short transaction, and still excludes the owners' `FOR UPDATE` changes. A `BASELINE` or `RECONCILE` version takes the revision before the change's, so that "the state at revision N" reads right.
- D-1049 (the agent, HIST-1). Content version 1: `ENTRY` {date, kind, payee, memo, externalRef, postings [account, currency, amount, category, counterparty]}; `ACCOUNT` {code, name, type, defaultCurrency, requiresCounterparty, system, archived}; `CATEGORY` and `FAMILY_CATEGORY` {code, name, type, archived}; `COUNTERPARTY` {name, kind, archived}; `FAMILY_RECORD` {type, date, category, payer, payee, amount (signed: a refund is negative), currency, splitMethod, comment, deleted, externalRef, shares [member, amount, basisPoints]}. Not in it: names, timestamps, the optimistic version, the import's stamps, the paying side, account, note and payee of the payer (D-16, D-1022). A family record that is deleted stays as a row with `deleted` true, so its version is a `DELETE` with the last content, and the integrity check reads the flag; a record whose row is gone gets a `DELETE` version holding its last content.
- D-1050 (the agent, HIST-1). The actor and cause of a version come from a frame on the thread (`HistoryContext`): `IMPORT` with the batch id (opened by `ImportService.run`, naming the user's sub, so the command line has an actor too), `DEMO`, `PROVISION`, `LEAVE` (the system's writes to a leaver's own ledger), `MERGE`, else `EDIT` with the signed-in user, whom `security.SecurityActorSource` gives (nothing in `ledger` but `ledger.api` may depend on Spring Security). The actor member is the member of that ledger with the sub; the kind is `SYSTEM` for `PROVISION`, `LEAVE`, `RETURN`, `BASELINE` and `RECONCILE`, `IMPORT` for an import, else `USER`. A dry run's versions and revision roll back with it.
- D-1051 (the agent, HIST-1). A member's departure (`CrossLedgerWriter.detach`) writes versions, cause `LEAVE`, actor `SYSTEM`, for what becomes the member's own: the copies of the family categories, the entries whose category moved (including the entries the family posted, which stop being derived) and the debt account, which turns into an ordinary liability. A return (`relinkDebt`, `reattach`) writes none: it only links again and the debt account is derived again, so it is no record, and its stale version is reconciled if the member leaves again.
- D-1052 (the agent, HIST-1, ADR 0006 section 8). `release_family_memberships` erases the leaver's comments from the versions with a window over each record's versions: a version's comment is the leaver's when the version where that value first appeared (the start of its run of equal values) was made by them; a run that starts at a `BASELINE` version belongs to whoever `family_record_change` says changed the comment last at the baseline's time. The version loses the key, which the canonical form reads as no comment; its stored hash is then stale, so the integrity check and the reconciling compare by recomputing the hash from the stored content where the stored one differs. The function also deletes the user's activity events by sub; `delete_family_ledger` deletes the ledger's versions and events; a personal ledger's cascade takes its versions. The immutability guard allows a delete under `app.writer` `delete-all` (these functions, `UserDataService.deleteAll`, the runbook's "Delete a user" SQL), under `history-purge` for activity events, and for a row whose ledger is gone.
- D-1053 (the agent, HIST-1). The integrity check of the history is `HistoryIntegrity.check(ledger)` and `GET /api/reports/integrity?history=true` (by default the answer is as before; it reads every record): a versioned record whose latest version differs from it, a record whose latest version is a delete but which is live, and a record whose row is gone and whose latest version isn't a delete. A record without a version is counted but not a fault until the baseline job has run (D-1020). The random tests run it after every operation, with the oracle (a copy of the ledger's live records every tenth operation, compared at the end with "the latest version of each record with a revision up to N").
- D-1054 (the agent, HIST-1). Only sign-in, sign-out and the end of a session are written as activity events in HIST-1 (`SIGN_IN`, `SIGN_OUT`, `SESSION_EXPIRED`, `SESSION_ENDED` with the reason `REFRESH_REFUSED`), by the login success handler, a logout handler that marks the session, the session listener and the refresh filter; the other log events of D-999 (settings, manual rates, memberships, invites, the family ledger's settings) are written from HIST-2 on, where something first reads them, and the privacy policy says only what is collected. A write that fails is logged and never fails the sign-in. Restarting the application ends every session with no listener call: the log cannot show those ends.
- D-1055 (the agent, HIST-1, D-1020, D-1042). `HistoryJobs` runs at startup in the background: the baseline (per ledger in its own transaction: a `BASELINE` version for every live record without one at the ledger's current revision, a `RECONCILE` for every one that differs from its latest, a `DELETE` for a record whose row is gone; idempotent), then the deletion of sign-in events older than 13 months, which also runs daily at 05:10 UTC (outside the forbidden windows of the deploy checklists); `app.history.jobs.enabled` turns both off, as the tests do (a test calls the job).
- D-1056 (the agent, HIST-1). The privacy policy's new section "History and sign-in events" (`#history`, after "Family budgets") and its retention bullet say only what HIST-1 collects: versions, the family record's history belonging to the budget, the leaver's name and comments, and the sign-in events with their 13 months; snapshots and notifications are added when they exist. The draft (`docs/family-budget/privacy-draft.md`) holds the text and `privacy.test.ts` compares it word for word. "Last updated" stays 18 November 2026, the stage's date; the owner moves it if the deploy is later.
- D-1057 (the agent, HIST-1). `deploy/checks/numbers.sql` gets three keys, `record_versions`, `activity_events` and `history_pending` (0 at rest), which take effect from the next deploy; `deploy/checks/HIST-1.sql` is WEB-1's 18 lines and V19's tables, columns, triggers and functions by name and argument types and invariants that hold whatever the application has written, with no count that moves by itself. What depends on the baseline job having run is the checklist's own block.
- D-1058 (the agent, HIST-1, D-1040). `rollback.sh` gets no V19 condition: after HIST-1 the rollback target is `d44df66` and it is unconditional (the previous image runs and writes normally on V19, proved by its own test suite run on a V19 database and by the drill on the rehearsal database). What the previous image writes while it runs has no versions; the baseline job of a later start and the lazy `RECONCILE` write them (D-1044).
- D-1059 (the agent, HIST-1). The tests that stand rows as older code left them (a raw `UPDATE` of an entry, an archived account with money, an importer's account for a sub without a `users` row) write through `LedgerApiTest.rawSql`, a connection that never says it is version-aware, like the previous image's; the tests' table lists and `OWNED_ROWS` name V19's tables (`record_version` for personal and for family ledgers apart, `activity_event`).
- D-1060 (the agent, HIST-1, D-1041 b). A record that the very write inserted needs no read: `BeforeConvertCallback` notes that the entity has no id, so the after-save callback calls `HistoryService.created` (an entry: its content from the saved entity, one insert) or `changed(…, fresh = true)` (no latest version and no committed state to read); `FamilyRecordService.journal` passes `fresh` for a `CREATE`. Reason: an import writes thousands of rows, and a read of the live record, of the latest version and of the committed state for each would spend D-1041's 20 % before any other work; what an existing record's first change costs (the committed read) stays. The import's timing is in the report.
- D-1061 (the agent, HIST-1; found by `e2e:local`). The committed state is read through a pool of its own (`HistoryService`: three connections, read only, never the application's), not in a nested `REQUIRES_NEW` transaction. The first version nested the read: every request holds a connection for its transaction and then asked the same pool (ten) for a second, so a page that loaded the demo while its other requests wrote hung until the pool timed out (`GET /api/entries/family-payees` never answered, and Playwright's `networkidle` waited for it: `themes` and `entries at 375 px` failed). The dedicated connections are held for one read and ask for nothing, so they can only wait, never deadlock. `HistoryApiTests.manyFirstChangesAtOnceDoNotStarveTheConnectionPool` runs thirty first changes at once; with the nested read it times out.
- D-1062 (the PM, HIST-1). The history job's startup scan stays as built: it runs in the background after `ApplicationReadyEvent`, and on later starts it reconciles records a previous image wrote without versions (after a rollback), which is worth its 20 seconds.
- D-1063 (the PM, HIST-1). `deploy/pg-backup/finance.conf`'s restore test also counts `record_version` and `activity_event`, and the checklist's expected lines follow.
- D-1064 (the PM, HIST-1). `history_pending` is empty outside a transaction: the history integrity check reports its row count, `HIST-1.sql` and `numbers.sql` check it, and the checklist checks it after `run` and after `e2e:prod`.
- D-1065 (the PM, HIST-1). "Last updated: 18 November 2026" stays (D-1056).
- D-1066 (the agent, HIST-1, D-1062). The health check `deploy.sh` waits for (`wait_healthy`) is the containers' own `docker inspect` health, from `/actuator/health` (the database and the disk); the baseline job runs on the scheduler's thread after the application is ready and takes no part in it, so a start is healthy while the job is still reading.
- D-1067 (the agent, HIST-1, D-1063). The two checks are appended to `finance.conf` (21 lines now); the file is installed by `run`'s postflight (4.5) and counts in the same postflight's restore test. The baseline writes versions for about a minute after the api starts and every sign-in writes an event, so a dump and a test that straddle either differ; the checklist says to dump and test again after the baseline's log line, and to send the output to the PM only if it differs twice.
- D-1068 (the agent, HIST-1, D-1064). `HistoryIntegrity.check` reports a committed mark of the ledger as a problem ("history_pending holds n committed marks"); the deferred check removes a mark or refuses the commit, so it takes the trigger switched off to leave one, which `HistoryApiTests` does for a moment. The checklist reads the count after the suite (step 10) and `verify HIST-1` prints it.
- D-1069 (the PM, HIST-1). The e2e flake is proved before the deploy: A/B runs of `e2e:local` on `d44df66` and on `8630df4`, each on a freshly built stack with a clean tree, with HikariCP's pool statistics and a sampling of `pg_stat_activity`.
- D-1070 (the agent, HIST-1, D-1069). The A/B result: `d44df66` passed 4 of 4 runs and `8630df4` failed 4 of 6 (HikariPool-1 never waited, no session waited on a lock but the first sign-in's `user_settings` upsert and, behind the stall, the cleanup's delete). The cause of the failures is one statement: the Entries monthly chart's first statement (`matched(...)` then `min(entry_date)`) ran 99 to 132 s on a freshly loaded demo, `auto_explain` showing the planner's estimate of 1 row for the 894 entries (`journal_entry` and its neighbours had statistics for no rows until autovacuum's next pass, after the previous spec's delete-all) and nested loops over it. The same statement and the same weakness are in `d44df66`, whose four runs did not meet it. Why HIST-1 meets it so much more often is not proven: the likely reason is that its extra writes (versions, the revision update) and tables delay autovacuum's analyze after a delete-all and a load, which widens the window; no run measured autovacuum's timing. The D-976 explanation was right about the mechanism, and wrong to call the flake not HIST-1's: HIST-1 makes it frequent, and the fix is D-1071's.
- D-1071 (the agent, HIST-1, D-1070). The fix is the import's own remedy: `DemoLedgerService.load` runs `ANALYZE` on the tables it filled once it has written everything (`journal_entry`, `posting`, `account`, `counterparty`, `category`, `family_record`, `family_share`, `family_entry_link`), in its transaction (ANALYZE is transactional), so a freshly loaded demo has the statistics of what it holds. The planner workaround of PERF-1 stays as it is; the monthly and breakdown statements are not given `enable_nestloop = off` (D-973: they are faster without it with statistics). With the fix `e2e:local` on a clean tree passed 3 of 3 runs, the slowest statement 1.5 to 2 s.
- D-1072 (the PM, after HIST-1's deploy). Maintenance package. (a) HIST-1's deploy is recorded from the owner's outputs (the runbook's row and "Production now", CLAUDE.md). (b) The postflight restore test's race with the baseline job is the deploy script's to absorb (D-1073). (c) The checklist's "entries 43919 or more" was wrong (production had 43,030) and says what does not go stale. (d) After "Delete all my data" no activity event may exist for that sub as a consequence of the same sign-out or session end: variant A (the app writes no sign-out, expiry or refused-refresh event for a sub that has just deleted its data); variant B, a purge, may be chosen instead by the owner.
- D-1073 (the agent, maintenance package, D-1072 b). The postflight restore test (`run`'s step 4.6) is tried twice, with no wait for the baseline's log line. Of the two ways offered (wait for the `History baseline:` line, or retry once), only the retry is built: it needs no knowledge of what runs in the api (a wait for that line would stall every deploy of a commit before HIST-1 and every deploy where the line is logged in an unexpected form, for the whole bound), it also covers any other writer that straddles a dump (a sign-in's event), and it is one place in the script. On a first FAIL `restore_test_after` keeps the table as `restore-test-after-first.txt`, waits `RESTORE_RETRY_WAIT` (`DEPLOY_RESTORE_RETRY_WAIT`, default 90 seconds: the baseline of 20,815 versions took 16.8 s), waits for another minute (D-43), takes a fresh dump and tests again; only a second FAIL ends the run `failed`. It prints which path it took. A retry that passes ends the run `deployed`, so `finish` works and `adopt` is not needed. The pre-flight tests (`run`'s 1.8, `db-image`'s) are unchanged, and so is `db-image`'s postflight (nothing runs in the background there). The wait variant's cases (line present, late, absent within a bound) therefore do not exist; the cases are: a first PASS (no retry), a first FAIL then PASS (`deployed`, `finish` works), two FAILs (`failed`), and two mutants.
- D-1074 (the agent, maintenance package, D-1072 d). Variant A as built: `SessionEvents` listens for `UserDataDeleted` (after the commit, as `CurrentUserResolver.forget` does) and keeps the sub in an in-memory set; a sign-out, a refused refresh and a session expiry of that sub write no event while it is in the set, and a sign-in removes it (and is logged as before). Chosen over "skip when the user has no `users` row" because a sign-in that makes no API request has no row and its sign-out must still be logged (`BrowserLoginTests` signs in and out that way), and over a purge, which would race the handler's own write. The set is in the JVM like the sessions; a restart forgets it with them. A user who deletes their data and then keeps using the same session is provisioned again but has no sign-out logged for that session until they sign in again: accepted, since it is the end of the session that the deletion was meant to leave nothing of. The privacy policy's "Delete all my data deletes them at once" stays accurate (and is now true of the sign-out that follows).
- D-1075 (PM, HIST-2 charter). `family_record_change` is not frozen and not dropped in HIST-2: HIST-1's dual write stays, so that a rollback to the previous image, which reads that table, keeps working. Only the reading of the family journal moves to versions; no migration in step 1.
- D-1076 (PM, HIST-2 charter). The log events of D-999 (settings, manual rates, ledger settings, memberships: joined, left, seat claimed, invites; SPLIT_RULE_RESET) are written in HIST-2 (step 3) and shown in a simple "Activity" list in part 2, next to the user's own sign-in, sign-out and expiry events.
- D-1077 (PM, HIST-2 charter). Notifications are informational only, no "Accept" (HIST-3): kinds FAMILY_CHANGES, FAMILY_IMPORTED, MEMBER_JOINED, MEMBER_LEFT and SEAT_CLAIMED; recipients are the other ACTIVE members, never the author; no text or content is stored (built when read); unread FAMILY_CHANGES from one author in one ledger merge, range widened (D-1034); the unread count is one cheap indexed statement, fetched on load and after navigation, never by an interval poll; everything family-related stays behind FAMILY_LEDGERS_ENABLED (step 4).
- D-1078 (PM, HIST-2 charter). History is shown as a side panel opened from a record (part 2); there is no "deleted records" list (HIST-4 brings restore). A personal record's history is for its owner only; a family record's for the ACTIVE members of that ledger only.
- D-1079 (PM, HIST-2 charter). The log events of D-999 are written in the same transaction as the change they describe (HIST-2 step 3).
- D-1080 (developer, HIST-2 step 1). The family journal's cut-over per record: the record's first version, if it is a BASELINE, is the cut-over point; journal rows written strictly before its time are shown, and a row written at the same instant (the lazy baseline's own transaction, where `now()` is equal) belongs to the versions, which describe the same change. A record whose first version is a CREATE shows versions only; a record without versions shows its journal rows only. A change read from a version carries the version's id negated, so it never equals a journal row's id in one page.
- D-1081 (manager, HIST-2 step 1). The family journal's URL, request parameters and answer do not change when it is read from versions (the owner-facing shape of D-1077 as first drafted). A create row read from a version names the currency only when it differs from the ledger's current main currency, not the one at the record's creation.
- D-1082 (manager, HIST-2 step 1). A change made by the previous image during a rollback, between versions, shows as one UPDATE diff into the RECONCILE baseline, with no author, at the reconcile's time. Accepted; criterion (a) of ADR section 13 is narrowed for such changes.
- D-1083 (manager, HIST-2 step 1). The journal of a comment that erasure removed reads the old table's own row of the same change (same record, same instant, same action, naming the comment field); a version diff with no comment field then adds "comment null→null". So the family journal reads `family_record_change` until the table is dropped; the drop's migration must leave the erasure its own mark in the version first.
- D-1084 (manager, HIST-2 step 1). A create row lists its shares in join order (the order of `members()`), not in the request order of an AMOUNT split, which no version stores. The shares and their amounts are the same; only the order differs. Owner to confirm.
- D-1085 (manager, HIST-2 step 1). A version whose previous version is missing (no version can be missing today: only whole-ledger erasure deletes versions) is skipped as a row, unless it is a CREATE, DELETE or ARCHIVE, instead of failing the request. Its change is then not shown: a known gap, to be closed before any partial deletion of versions.
- D-1086 (manager, HIST-2 step 2). One history endpoint per record kind, `GET /api/history/{recordType}/{recordId}`, paged; a personal record's history is for its owner only (404 for anyone else), a family record's for ACTIVE members of its ledger only, a non-member answering exactly as a missing family record (404; D-1093), and off behind the family switch.
- D-1087 (manager, HIST-2 step 2). The notification table holds no text and no content; a notification row is a reference (kind, author, the revision range, the count); its text is built when read (step 4 builds the text).
- D-1088 (manager, HIST-2 step 2). The unread count is one indexed statement over the partial index on unread rows; the frontend fetches it on load and after navigation, never by a timer (part 2 decides the client side).
- D-1089 (developer, HIST-2 step 2). The personal delete-all deletes notifications inside `release_family_memberships`, where V19 deletes `activity_event`: by sub through `ledger_member` before the rows lose the sub, and per membership after the lock. `delete_family_ledger` deletes the ledger's notifications explicitly, in V19's order. Same `app.writer` handling; the rest of each function is V19's text.
- D-1090 (developer, HIST-2 step 2). `notification` has no immutability trigger, since `read_at` must be updatable; its erasure needs no guard, and the functions still set the writer as V19 does.
- D-1091 (developer, HIST-2 step 2). The author's foreign key is `ON DELETE CASCADE`, as the spec says. Member rows are never deleted today, only marked FORMER.
- D-1092 (developer, HIST-2 step 2). `notification` has the CHECKs `from_revision >= 0`, `changes_count >= 0` and `to_revision >= from_revision`, and indexes on (recipient, created_at), ledger_id and author_member_id beside the required partial index on unread rows.
- D-1093 (developer, HIST-2 step 2). A family record's history has no ledger id in its path: the API looks through the caller's ACTIVE family budgets for the one holding the record's versions, each lookup scoped to that budget's ledger. A non-member and a missing record both answer 404 "Family record N not found", not "Family budget N not found", since no budget id is known.
- D-1094 (developer, HIST-2 step 2). An unknown `recordType`, FAMILY_CATEGORY, and a FAMILY_RECORD history with the family switch off all answer like an unknown path (404 by NoResourceFoundException). The personal history endpoint works whatever the switch.
- D-1095 (developer, HIST-2 step 2). The answer is `{content, page, size, total, totalPages}`; each version has `seq`, `revision`, `op`, `at`, `cause`, `actor{kind, memberId, name}` and `changes`. `name` is null for SYSTEM and IMPORT and "Former member" for a FORMER or missing member. Personal changes are `[{field, old, new}]` over the top-level keys of the canonical content, values and ids as stored; a first version lists every key with old null.
- D-1096 (developer, HIST-2 step 2). A family version reuses `FamilyJournalDiff` and the journal's own change-building code, so names resolve exactly as in the journal. Unlike the journal, every version is listed: a BASELINE with no earlier version reads as a creation, and a version with no visible change has empty `changes`. The erased-comment rule matches the journal's.
- D-1097 (developer, HIST-2 step 2). The unread count runs one SQL statement (notification joined to `ledger_member` by sub and ACTIVE status, `read_at IS NULL`) in `ledger.history.Notifications`, which is added to the ArchitectureTests list of classes allowed SQL keyed by the user's sub.
- D-1098 (manager, HIST-2 step 3). The activity list (`GET /api/activity?page=&size=`, default 50, max 200) shows the caller's own events (`activity_event` rows whose actor is the caller's sub) and the events of the family ledgers where the caller is an ACTIVE member. Nothing else. Behind the family switch only for the ledger events; the caller's own sign-in, sign-out and expiry events show whatever the switch.
- D-1099 (manager, HIST-2 step 3). SPLIT_RULE_RESET is written to `activity_event` in addition to the `family_record_change` row it already writes (dual write, as D-1075), so the journal's reading of system changes is unchanged; the activity list shows it from `activity_event` only.
- D-1100 (manager, HIST-2 step 3). When a member's data is erased (`release_family_memberships`, V19's and V20's pattern), the leaver's own ledger events (actor sub) are deleted in the same function; other members' events about the leaver (for example "X joined") are kept, with the actor's name shown as "Former member" at read time. Keep the functions' signatures and old callers' behaviour (D-1040); a replaced function's text changes only by the delete.
- D-1101 (developer, HIST-2 step 3). No V21: `activity_event.kind` has no CHECK, and V20's `release_family_memberships` already deletes every row whose actor is the user's sub (ledger rows included) and "Delete all my data" calls it, so D-1100 holds without a change to the erasure functions.
- D-1102 (developer, HIST-2 step 3). Events name members by id only (`subject_type` MEMBER and `subject_id`, the actor by sub), never by name: the list resolves names at read time (the member's name now, "Former member" for a FORMER or missing member), so an erased member's name stays nowhere. Each row of the list carries, in addition to the fields D-1098's step names, `subject {memberId, name}` for a membership's event (null otherwise). Join events (MEMBER_JOINED, SEAT_CLAIMED) are written with the joiner as their actor, so they go with the joiner's own erasure; the events kept about a leaver are other users' (MEMBER_LEFT, SPLIT_RULE_RESET, INVITE_CREATED for their seat). Owner to confirm.
- D-1103 (developer, HIST-2 step 3). Kinds: SETTINGS_CHANGED (`details.changes`: `{field, old, new}` of each changed field of the settings, time zone or date format), MANUAL_RATE_SET, MANUAL_RATE_CHANGED and MANUAL_RATE_REMOVED (`date`, `currency`, `old`, `new`; a CSV upload writes one per changed rate), LEDGER_SETTINGS_CHANGED (name, base currency, split rule and shares by member id), MEMBER_JOINED and SEAT_CLAIMED (the invite's use: `inviteId`, `returned`), MEMBER_LEFT (`removed`: by an owner or by themselves), INVITE_CREATED (`inviteId`, `seatMemberId`, `hours`; never the token), INVITE_REVOKED and SPLIT_RULE_RESET. A save that changes nothing writes none. Not logged: the creation of a family budget, a declined invite, the invites revoked by a member's leaving, and FORMER marking and resets done inside `release_family_memberships`.
- D-1104 (developer, HIST-2 step 3). SPLIT_RULE_RESET is shown with actor kind SYSTEM (no member, no name), like a version's LEAVE cause; its row keeps the acting user's sub, so it goes with that user's erasure. Every other event is the acting user's (USER); a person-level event (no ledger) has no member and no name. A member who left still sees their own ledger events (they are the actor), but no other event of that family budget.
- D-1105 (manager, HIST-2 step 4). A notification is written in the same transaction as the change it describes (as D-1079 for events). A change that fails writes no notification; a notification insert that fails rolls the change back.
- D-1106 (manager, HIST-2 step 4). The merge: an unread FAMILY_CHANGES for the same recipient, ledger and author is updated in place (`from_revision` the smaller, `to_revision` the larger, `changes_count` added, `created_at` refreshed) instead of a new row. A read one is never changed; a new row is then created. Two transactions merging the same row must not lose a count.
- D-1107 (manager, HIST-2 step 4). Reads and marks: `GET /api/notifications?page=&size=` (default 50, max 200, newest first) returns `{content, page, size, total, totalPages}`; each row has id, kind, at, read, author `{memberId, name}` (name at read time, "Former member" for a FORMER or missing member), a revision range and a text built when read from the kind, the author's name and the count. `POST /api/notifications/{id}/read` marks one of the caller's (another user's or a missing one: 404); `POST /api/notifications/read-all` marks the caller's unread rows. Only rows of ledgers where the caller is ACTIVE are returned or marked. With the family switch off all three answer 404 like an unknown path.
- D-1108 (manager, HIST-2 step 4). An actor is never notified of their own change; the recipients are fixed when the notification is written (members ACTIVE at that instant), and no notification is written for a ledger that is not family-enabled.
- D-1109 (developer, HIST-2 step 4). No V21: V20's `notification` table has every column and CHECK needed, and its erasure functions are unchanged (`release_family_memberships` deletes the rows a user received; `delete_family_ledger` and the cascades take the rest).
- D-1110 (developer, HIST-2 step 4). The merge is one atomic statement in `Notifications.familyChanged`: a data-modifying CTE `UPDATE notification ... SET changes_count = changes_count + 1 ... WHERE read_at IS NULL RETURNING recipient_member_id`, then an INSERT for the recipients it did not update. A concurrent merge waits for the row lock and PostgreSQL re-evaluates the row after it (READ COMMITTED), so the count is never lost; a row marked read meanwhile no longer matches and a new row is created. Writers of one ledger are also serialized by the ledger's row lock. Tested with two transactions on one row.
- D-1111 (developer, HIST-2 step 4). Recipients are ACTIVE members with an account (`user_sub` set) of a SHARED ledger, other than the actor and the author; a place without an account gets none. The write is skipped when `app.family.enabled` is off. FAMILY_CHANGES counts one change per create, update, delete or settlement call and takes the ledger revision that call's transaction took (HIST-1); a call that took none (nothing changed) writes nothing. The import's own record writes (`*ForImport`) write no FAMILY_CHANGES.
- D-1112 (developer, HIST-2 step 4). Membership kinds: the author is the member the event is about (who joined, left, was removed or claimed a seat), the actor is who did it (an owner, for a removal); neither is notified. MEMBER_LEFT covers both leaving and removal (no content says which). They carry no revision range and no count. A member without an account whose row is deleted on removal takes the notification about them with it (the author's foreign key cascades).
- D-1113 (developer, HIST-2 step 4). FAMILY_IMPORTED is written once per committed import that names a family budget, at the end of the import's transaction, with the revision the import took on that budget, or no range when it changed only the importer's own side of the records. A dry run or a refused import writes none.
- D-1114 (developer, HIST-2 step 4). Texts (English, built in the pure class `NotificationText`): "{name} made a change in the family budget", "{name} made {n} changes in the family budget", "{name} imported records into the family budget", "{name} joined the family budget", "{name} left the family budget", "{name} took their place in the family budget"; "Someone" when there is no author. Each row also returns its `ledgerId` and its `changesCount`. Marking a read row again keeps its first `read_at`.
- D-1115 (PM, HIST-2 1b step 2) amends D-1113: FAMILY_IMPORTED is written only when the import took a revision on the family budget, at that revision. An import that changed only the importer's own side of the records, or nothing, notifies nobody; a second import of the same file writes none.
- D-1116 (PM, HIST-2 1b step 2): a version skipped under D-1085 (its previous version is missing, it is past seq 1, and it is not a CREATE, DELETE or ARCHIVE; a seq 1 BASELINE is no gap and is not logged) is logged at WARN with the ledger id, the record id and the version's seq, no content; `HistoryIntegrity`'s report has no place for such a count, so only the log.
- D-1117 (developer, HIST-2 1b step 2): the journal's count query finds the skipped versions in the same scan (its `changes` set keeps them, flagged, and the page query leaves them out), so a healthy read runs no extra statement; the WARN is written on every journal read that meets the gap, once per skipped version. The record history (`RecordHistory`) is not logged.
- D-1118 (PM, HIST-2 part 2 step 1) a "History" action on a record opens a side panel (a full-screen sheet on a phone below the existing breakpoint), no new top-level page: on Entries rows (and in the entry editor for a saved entry), accounts, categories, payees (counterparties), and family records. It lists versions newest first, with load-more paging: date and time in the user's date format and time zone (the existing helpers in `dates.ts` and `timeZone.ts`, never the browser's clock: no `new Date()` for display), who (the actor's name; "Import" and "System" for those kinds), what happened (Created, Changed, Deleted, Archived, Restored, and "State when history began" for the baseline), the cause when it is an import, demo, merge and so on, and the field changes as "field: old → new" with human labels, money formatted as elsewhere (`formatMoney`, Big.js, never JS numbers). No decision numbers and no technical words in screen text. A record that has no history (404) says so plainly in the panel. Colours only from theme tokens. Payees are not in this step: see D-1124.
- D-1119 (PM, HIST-2 part 2 step 1) the Activity page is reached from the user's name menu (next to Settings and Log out), titled "Activity": a paged list of the user's own sign-ins, sign-outs, ended sessions, settings and rates changes and, when the user is in a family budget, its membership and settings events, each with a plain sentence built from the row, the time, and a note that restarts end sessions without a sign-out event (ADR 2.3). Names as the API returns them.
- D-1120 (PM, HIST-2 part 2 step 1) the notification badge is in the header next to the user's menu, shows the unread count (hidden at 0, "99+" over 99), is fetched on page load and after each navigation or after the user marks something read, never by a timer; with the family switch off the badge and the notifications page do not exist. The list page ("Notifications") shows the text the API returns, the time, unread marked, a "Mark all as read" button; opening an item marks it read and goes to the family journal (kinds FAMILY_CHANGES, FAMILY_IMPORTED) or the members tab (membership kinds) of that ledger. No "Accept" while acceptance is off; amended in HIST-3 part 5 (D-1184, D-1185, D-1190): with acceptance on, rows of the kinds FAMILY_CHANGES, FAMILY_IMPORTED and FAMILY_RESTORED get an "Accept" button while the member has pending changes in that ledger, and the family pending count is never fetched by a timer.
- D-1121 (PM, HIST-2 part 2 step 1) the privacy page gets: the history of records (what a version holds, that it is kept, that "Delete all my data" erases it), the activity events and their 13 months, the notifications (in the app only, no content, deleted with you or when you leave); every promise of the existing sections stays true.
- D-1122 (manager, HIST-2 part 2 step 1) the panel's open state and its loaded pages are component state, not URL state; opening it does not change the page's URL. It is a dialog over the page, not a page the user shares.
- D-1123 (manager, HIST-2 part 2 step 1) on Entries the History action is in each row's existing action menu (or beside the row's actions, whichever matches the screen's pattern); in the entry editor it is offered only for a saved entry, never for a new one. Entries' data loading, summary and statements do not change; only the action and the panel are added.
- D-1124 (manager, HIST-2 part 2 step 1) the payee History action waits for a screen that lists payees as rows; none exists today (the Your payees tab holds a checkbox and members). The payee action comes with a payees screen, which this step does not build (manager decision, accepted by the PM).
- D-1125 (manager, HIST-2 part 2 step 1) load-more: 20 versions per page, a "Load more" button, hidden when all versions are loaded; while a page loads the button is disabled and shows a loading text; an error on load-more keeps the loaded versions and shows the error with a retry.
- D-1126 (manager, HIST-2 part 2 step 1) field labels are a fixed map per record kind, written in the app's plain language (the same words the form uses for the field). An unknown field name shows as "Other detail" with its value, never the raw key. Values: money as `formatMoney` in its currency; dates as `formatDate` in the user's format; booleans as "Yes"/"No"; null as an em dash "—"; a reference that is an id the frontend cannot name shows as "Removed item" (not the id).
- D-1127 (manager, HIST-2 part 2 step 1) cause labels: a fixed map from the API's cause values to plain text (import, demo data, merge, and the rest as the API defines them); an unknown cause shows as "Other change". Op labels: CREATE "Created", UPDATE "Changed", DELETE "Deleted", ARCHIVE "Archived", RESTORE "Restored", BASELINE "State when history began".
- D-1128 (manager, HIST-2 part 2 step 1) on a phone (below 40rem, the app's existing breakpoint) the panel is a full-screen sheet with its own close button; above it, a right-side panel. Nothing scrolls sideways at 375 px.
- D-1154 (PM, HIST-3) HIST-3 is developed now and deployed only if accepted and verified in production by 2026-11-28; otherwise it is deployed after the December import.
- D-1155 (manager, HIST-3 part 1) the counters are three additive columns and no index: `ledger_member.accepted_revision` (BIGINT NULL, set for ACTIVE members with an account only), `family_record.last_revision` (BIGINT NOT NULL DEFAULT 0), `family_entry_link.posted_revision` (BIGINT NOT NULL DEFAULT 0). No new index: the counters are read only by the integrity check and by HIST-3's later parts; no read statement changes.
- D-1156 (manager, HIST-3 part 1) the acceptance switch is the property `app.family.acceptance.enabled` (environment `FAMILY_ACCEPTANCE_ENABLED`, default false), read by a bean `FamilyAcceptanceSwitch` next to `FamilySwitch`. In this part nothing branches on it; it only logs its state at startup.
- D-1157 (manager, HIST-3 part 1) the backfill in V21: `accepted_revision` := the ledger's revision for each ACTIVE member with an account; `last_revision` := the revision of the record's latest version, or 0 when it has no version; `posted_revision` := its record's `last_revision`. After the migration nothing is pending.
- D-1158 (manager, HIST-3 part 1) join, claim and return set `accepted_revision` to the ledger's head at that transaction (after its lock); leave and becoming FORMER set it to null. A deleted record has no row, so it has no counter; its links are removed or re-posted by the change set that deletes it.
- D-1159 (PM, HIST-3 part 2) pending is computed from records, never from gaps in revision numbers: record R is pending for member m when R.last_revision > accepted_revision(m) (equivalently: no non-detached link and last_revision > accepted_revision, or a non-detached link with posted_revision < last_revision). A revision that changes no record (a category rename and the other label-only changes of D-1014) advances accepted_revision ONLY for members whose accepted_revision equals the previous head (revision - 1); lagging members keep theirs. With the switch off, everybody advances at each revision, as before.
- D-1160 (PM, HIST-3 part 2) an idempotent service method re-runs the V21 backfill (counters from record_version, links, and accepted_revision := head for ACTIVE members with an account). It refuses, with a clear error, while the switch is on, or when any family ledger has a member whose accepted_revision is below head. The deploy script uses it before the switch is turned on (part 4: the rollback to the V20 image writes no counters).
- D-1161 (manager, HIST-3 part 2) a family record change set (create, edit, delete, settle) with the switch on posts only into the actor's books (and, for a settlement, the actor's side) and only if the actor is caught up; it sets last_revision = R on the record, posted_revision = R on the actor's links, and accepted_revision = R for the actor. Other members are not touched; their records become pending by D-1159.
- D-1162 (manager, HIST-3 part 2) the author rule: an actor who is not caught up and tries to create, edit, delete or settle a family record gets 409 with the stable code `FAMILY_PENDING_CHANGES` and the count of pending records, in the message "accept the n pending changes first". The count is one indexed statement, in the reusable method `pendingRecords(ledger, member)`, which part 3 uses for Accept. Detached links (detached_at set) are ignored; members without an account accept nothing and are never pending. The 409 offers "Accept" in the same place only in part 4 (D-1027).
- D-1163 (manager, HIST-3 part 2) a deleted family record is recognised by its soft delete (deleted_at set, V7) and its last_revision, which the delete moves like any change; no new table. Other members' links to it are pending by D-1159 until they accept (part 3).
- D-1164 (manager, HIST-3 part 2) an Excel import commit by a lagging actor goes through the same posting path and gets the same 409 (`FAMILY_PENDING_CHANGES`); the importer's own refusal text is part 3 and is not written here.
- D-1165 (manager, HIST-3 part 2) the reconcile of D-1160 (`AcceptanceCounters`) refuses on the counters as stored, before it recomputes them: a member with a record whose last_revision is above their accepted_revision, or an attached link posted below its record's last_revision. The recomputed counters would refuse the rollback case itself (the V20 image writes no counter, so links look stale), and the stored check still refuses real lag from a switch-on period. The importer's commit (D-1164) takes the author rule's check under the ledger lock at its start, before any row or category is written, so a lagging uploader gets 409 FAMILY_PENDING_CHANGES.
- D-1166 (PM, HIST-3 part 3) HIST-3 deploys dark: the existing e2e specs 03, 04, 05, 07 and 09 stay on switch OFF and unchanged; adapting them is the later "turn acceptance on in production" step, not part of HIST-3. Part 4 adds one new local spec and the deploy script's switch.
- D-1167 (PM proposal, HIST-3 part 3) a successful accept marks the accepting member's unread FAMILY_CHANGES, FAMILY_IMPORTED and FAMILY_RESTORED notifications of that ledger with to_revision at most the head he accepted as read. Nothing else about notifications changes.
- D-1168 (manager, HIST-3 part 3) the Accept action (POST /api/family-ledgers/{ledgerId}/accept): one transaction, the family ledger's row lock first; for the calling ACTIVE member with an account only; re-posts into his books only every record with a pending change (D-1159), created, edited, deleted (soft: his posting is removed as a normal delete does) or settled; re-runs his openings; sets posted_revision on his links and then accepted_revision to the ledger's current revision. It takes no ledger revision. Idempotent: a caught-up member gets 200 with 0. Returns the number of records accepted. With the switch off the endpoint answers 404 as an unknown path does.
- D-1169 (manager, HIST-3 part 3) the pending marks on the family records list and detail: pendingForMe is computed by one batched statement per page, over the page's record ids and the caller's member row; with the switch off it is false and no statement runs.
- D-1170 (manager, HIST-3 part 3) the pending summary (GET /api/family-ledgers/{ledgerId}/pending) answers the count and the ids of the caller's pending records; with the switch off it answers 404 as an unknown path does.
- D-1171 (manager, HIST-3 part 3) the integrity report with the switch on: a member with pending records shows pendingChanges with its count and his debt-versus-family-balance comparison is skipped; a caught-up member is compared as before. With the switch off the output is byte-for-byte as now.
- D-1172 (manager, HIST-3 part 3) the importer's dry run by a lagging uploader lists the pending changes as a named note, which does not fail the run: "N family changes are waiting for you; accept them before committing". The commit refusal of D-1164 is unchanged.
- D-1173 (PM, HIST-3 part 4) the lagging uploader's dry run lists ONE named non-failing note ("N family changes are waiting for you; accept them before committing", D-1172) and NO per-row errors for family rows caused by the author rule. The family rows that would have been checked are reported in the run's summary as a count, worded "not checked until you accept". The run stays DRY_RUN and writes nothing. The commit refusal (409, FAMILY_PENDING_CHANGES, D-1164) is unchanged.
- D-1174 (PM, HIST-3 part 4) the acceptance UI is part 5, not part 4. HIST-3 deploys dark without it.
- D-1175 (manager, HIST-3 part 4) `deploy/deploy.sh acceptance on|off` copies `switch`'s preflight: `on` refuses on health, a HEAD that isn't the last good deploy, images that aren't its content and unfinished work; `off` prints each of them as a WARNING and goes on, as `switch off` does. Unlike `switch off` (D-50), both directions need the read-only role, since both read the pending count. It reads neither the pages nor the numbers: the restart changes no data and no page.
- D-1176 (manager, HIST-3 part 4) the reconcile is the manual SQL file `deploy/manual/reconcile-acceptance.sql` (V21's backfill as `AcceptanceCounters` reruns it, with counts before and after, refusing while anything is pending by the stored counters). The operator never runs the file: the runbook's block ("Family acceptance (HIST-3)") holds its text in a heredoc after a guard that stops unless `FAMILY_ACCEPTANCE_ENABLED` in `.env` is absent or `false`, and a fresh backup, run as the app's login `finance` like "Delete a user". `deploy/tests/run.sh` checks that the block is the file byte for byte; the file was tried on a throwaway database migrated by Flyway to V21.
- D-1177 (manager, HIST-3 part 4) `acceptance off` refuses while any record is pending ("accept first", with the count) and also when the count can't be read (no role, the check failing, or an unexpected line).
- D-1178 (manager, HIST-3 part 4) `verify` and `finish` print one line about family acceptance (the `.env` line and the api's logged line), for information only: never a problem, never a stop, so their verdicts don't change.
- D-1179 (manager, HIST-3 part 4) the pre-check is `deploy/checks/acceptance.sql`, a check file without an `.expected` (like `numbers.sql`): one line `acceptance members=<m> links=<l> pending=<p>` that `deploy.sh` parses; `on` needs all three 0 and the family switch on in `.env` and in the running api. `numbers.sql` gets no key (V21 adds no table). The stage check `HIST-3.sql` is HIST-2's lines with the Flyway line at V21, then V21's columns, its trigger by name and four invariants of the counters that hold with acceptance off.
- D-1180 (manager, HIST-3 part 4) D-1173's count is `totals.notCheckedUntilAccepted` of the import report (also in the stored run summary, and one line of the Markdown report when above 0); it counts each family row whose write the author rule refused in a dry run, including a payment side and each row of a grouped settlement.
- D-1181 (manager, HIST-3 part 4) detached family links (a member who left, `detached_at` set) are never stamped again (`CrossLedgerWriter` stamps attached links only), so the pre-check `deploy/checks/acceptance.sql`, the invariant of `HIST-3.sql` and the reconcile's counts before and after count attached links only (`detached_at IS NULL`). The reconcile's UPDATE of the links stays V21's (and `AcceptanceCounters`'): over every link, detached ones included, setting each to its record's `last_revision`; nothing reads a detached link's counter, and a record the leaver is in is frozen, so it doesn't move afterwards. `FamilyCountersApiTests.aDetachedLinkIsNotCountedByTheStageCheck` runs HIST-3's query after a leave and the remaining member's change sets.

- D-1183 (PM, HIST-3 deploy) the HIST-3 dark deploy needed no manual browser checks: the end-to-end suite on production (`e2e:prod`, 2026-11-25) replaced them, as the deploy checklist's step 4 says. Recorded in the "Deployed revisions" row of b66115f in `deploy/RUNBOOK.md`, by the first commit of HIST-3 part 5 (`docs(runbook): record the HIST-3 deploy of b66115f`).
- D-1184 (PM, HIST-3 part 5) after a successful Accept the UI never retries the refused action by itself: it keeps what the user typed, says how many changes were accepted, and the user presses the same button again.
- D-1185 (PM, HIST-3 part 5) the UI learns that acceptance is on by feature detection: a successful answer of a budget's pending summary means on, a 404 (unknown path) means off. No new setting for users. The answer is kept for the session, "off" included, so with acceptance off nothing is asked again and nothing is drawn; with the family budget off the family pages are not drawn, so nothing is asked at all.
- D-1186 (manager, HIST-3 part 5) the detection (`acceptance.ts`, `askPending`) is a module-level answer of the page's session. A 404 counts as "off" only when asked for a budget the user currently belongs to (a family page that has loaded, or a budget of the shell's list), since a budget the user left answers 404 too; any other failure (network, 5xx) changes nothing and only hides the banner until a later answer.
- D-1187 (manager, HIST-3 part 5) the pending count of a budget is asked when one of its pages opens and on each move between its pages (the pathname, as the unread badge does), and after an Accept; never by a timer, focus or visibility. The banner reads "1 change by others is waiting for you" / "3 changes by others are waiting for you" with a button "Accept", above every page of the budget, only above 0. After the banner's Accept it says "Accepted 3 changes." ("Nothing was waiting for you." for 0) and the Overview's latest activity, the Activity list and an open record load again.
- D-1188 (manager, HIST-3 part 5) the mark is the badge "Waiting for you" (the stale colour of the themes) on a record with `pendingForMe`, in the list and on the record's page, with the title "The record shows its latest version. Your own books follow once you accept the change." (on the page also as a note). Reports and balances do not change (ADR 0006 section 7.2).
- D-1189 (manager, HIST-3 part 5) a refused write (409, code `FAMILY_PENDING_CHANGES`) shows the server's message and, in the same box, the button "Accept N change(s)" (N from the 409's `pendingChanges`); after the click the box says "Accepted N change(s). Nothing was saved yet. Press the same button again." and the form is untouched. It stands in the place of the action's other errors on the new expense, income and settlement forms, the record page's edit and delete, the payer's own payment entry (personal page), the personal editor's family expense option, and the import's commit (which accepts the budget the form names). No other error gets a button.
- D-1190 (manager, HIST-3 part 5) the notifications list asks the pending count per budget, one request at a time and only for budgets of the shell's list that have a row of kind FAMILY_CHANGES, FAMILY_IMPORTED or FAMILY_RESTORED on the page, and stops at the first "off"; each such row, read or not, gets a button "Accept" (title "Accept N changes") beside the row while its budget has pending changes. The click accepts, then the list reloads, the header badge asks again and the counts are asked again; the server marks the covered rows read (D-1167). With acceptance off, or no such row, nothing is asked.
- D-1191 (manager, HIST-3 part 5) the import page shows the dry run's named note (D-1172) as a notice above the totals, besides the warnings list, with no button of its own, and, only above 0, "N family rows not checked until you accept" from `totals.notCheckedUntilAccepted`.
- D-1192 (manager, HIST-3 part 5) the end-to-end `Watch` lets a 404 of `GET /api/family-ledgers/{id}/pending` pass without naming it, since that answer is the app's own detection of acceptance off (D-1185) and every family spec with acceptance off would otherwise fail on it; any other 4xx or 5xx still fails. `18-acceptance.spec.ts` runs only locally and only where that summary answers 200; the runner has no way to run one spec alone, so the local run with acceptance on is for that spec's result, and the other specs stay written for acceptance off (D-1166).
- D-1193 (PM, HIST-4 part 1) snapshots are backend only and for personal ledgers only; a family ledger's snapshot request is refused with a clear error (409 `FAMILY_SNAPSHOTS_UNSUPPORTED`) until HIST-5.
- D-1194 (PM, HIST-4 part 1) the snapshot migration is V22; the restore tables come with V23 in part 2; HIST-3b, if ever built, takes the next free number (ADR 0006 section 9).
- D-1195 (PM, HIST-4 part 1) no delete and no rename of a snapshot in this part; a safety cap of 1,000 snapshots per ledger, refused with 409 `SNAPSHOT_LIMIT_REACHED`.
- D-1196 (manager, HIST-4 part 1) a snapshot locks the ledger row with `SELECT ... FOR SHARE`: it waits for a writer that holds the row's `FOR UPDATE` until that writer commits, does not block plain readers, and two snapshots do not block each other; the revision is read after the lock.
- D-1197 (manager, HIST-4 part 1) the cap check and the insert run after that lock, not under a stronger one; two snapshots made at once can both pass a count of 999, and that race is accepted, since the cap is a safety limit.
- D-1198 (manager, HIST-4 part 1) the list is paged by offset: default size 50, maximum 200 (400 above that), newest first.
- D-1199 (manager, HIST-4 part 1) an absent or blank name becomes "Snapshot yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm" in the user's time zone (UTC when none is set), no seconds; the name is trimmed and counts 1 to 80 UTF-16 characters, and a control character is refused with 400 `SNAPSHOT_NAME_INVALID`.
- D-1200 (manager, HIST-4 part 1) the create endpoint writes kind MANUAL only; BEFORE_IMPORT and BEFORE_RESTORE are accepted by the schema and the reads, and no code path writes them yet.
- D-1201 (manager, HIST-4 part 1) the consistency check (live records per type against the state at N, 409 `HISTORY_INCONSISTENT`) runs in the same transaction, after the lock, and only refuses; it does not repair.
- D-1202 (PM, HIST-4 part 1) `snapshot.kind` has no CHECK constraint, as `activity_event.kind` (D-1101), so later kinds need no migration; the application accepts MANUAL, BEFORE_IMPORT and BEFORE_RESTORE, and only MANUAL is written in part 1 (D-1200).
- D-1203 (manager, TEST-1a) Backend tests share one Spring context per configuration and one Postgres container per JVM. No test is deleted, disabled or weakened; the test count stays 859. Context keys come from the same annotations; `@DirtiesContext` is not used for isolation, data is isolated by rollback or by a cleanup of the application tables that keeps Flyway's history (the migrations seed no reference data) data. The Postgres container is started once per JVM and may be reused locally only with `testcontainers.reuse.enable=true`; CI behaves as before.
- D-1204 (manager, TEST-1b) The random tests (`*Random*`, tagged `random`) take their volume from the system property `tests.random.scale` (default 1, full volume: today's numbers, which CI runs). The quick local scale is 0.1. A run at a scale below 1 or with a seed override skips the end-of-test fingerprint of the random mix (the count that the full seed produced) and scales the other volume thresholds of the test; no iteration check changes. Every random test prints its seed on failure and takes `tests.random.seed` to replay it.
