- D-1205 (manager, TEST-1b, D-T5) The backend tests run in surefire forks (`tests.forks`, default 3, `reuseForks`): each fork is its own JVM with its own Postgres container. Parallelism is by forks only; JUnit's in-JVM parallel execution is not used, because its threads would share one database. Measured on a 12-core, 15 GB laptop, full suite: 23 min 37 s at 1 fork, 10 min 23 s at 2, 6 min 49 s at 3 (repeats 6 min 59 s and 6 min 49 s, all 859 passing). A reused container serves fork 1 only (`tests.fork`): the static setup of each fork empties its tables, so one container can serve one fork. CI runs the default; its runner is not measured, and `-Dtests.forks=<n>` overrides it there.
- D-1206 (manager, TEST-1b, D-T2) The strict reviewer does not run tests; it reviews the diff with the manager's run summary (tier, command, exit status, counts, wall time). A developer runs only the targeted tests for what it touched, never the full or the quick tier and never the e2e suite. The manager runs the quick tier once per task after the developer reports done, and the full tier only when the task changes test infrastructure or random-test code. Local Playwright runs only for UI flows it covers; never the production project.
- D-1207 (manager, CI-1, D-T7) The Mutations step of the job Deploy scripts (`deploy/tests/mutate.sh`) runs only when the pushed range changes one of: `deploy/deploy.sh`, `deploy/rollback.sh`, `deploy/common.sh`, anything under `deploy/tests/`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `.github/scripts/mutations-needed.sh`, `.github/scripts/tests/mutations-needed-test.sh`. Otherwise the step logs and records in the step summary "Mutations skipped: no deploy-script change since <short BEFORE>" and passes. The decision is `.github/scripts/mutations-needed.sh BEFORE AFTER` (exit 0 run, 1 skip; it runs when there is no BEFORE, on a new branch, when BEFORE or AFTER is not in the history or when git fails, and it looks at the net difference of the range). ShellCheck, Tests (`deploy/tests/run.sh`), the helper's test and Monitor's tests run on every push. The job keeps its 5 check runs: no job-level `if`, no `paths` filter (D-95). The manual workflow `ci-ubuntu-26.yml` (`workflow_dispatch` only) keeps running its Mutations step always.
- D-1219 (Sergey, HIST-4 part 2) an entry that a restore writes counts as untouched since its import (import_version = version; a re-inserted one gets import_hash 'restored', matching no row), so the next import writes the file's content over it when the file differs: Excel stays the source (D-1038); restoring an imported entry in the app lasts until the next import of a file that says otherwise.

## Planned stages
- F1: analysis and ADR 0003 (done).
- F2a: migration V5, stronger isolation tests, these documents; deployed on its own.
- F2b: LedgerScope and LedgerAccess, every query scoped by ledger_id, an architecture test; deployed on its own.
- F3a: migration V6 and the backend: family ledgers, members without accounts, split rule, family categories in the family ledger, the feature switch (D-25), and the membership part of D-20.
- F3b: the interface: the ledger switcher and the family pages.
- F4a: the backend of family expense records and posting: family records, shares, the posting service, expenses in the base currency, balances, read-only posted rows, the change journal, the start date (D-27); UNALLOCATED becomes a system account. Also family categories in personal ledgers: merging the creator's chosen categories into the family ones at creation, the posting trigger's exception for family categories, and family categories in personal category lists and reports. Until then a family category lives only in its family ledger.
- F4b: the interface of family expense records.
- F4c: the payer's side, backend and interface: edits of the payment fields (D-14), moving a payment to an account, the payer's own payment entry through the personal endpoints, and marking a new personal expense as family (C2). Until F4c, a wrong payment field is fixed by deleting the record and entering it again.
- F4d: settlements (D2, D-24) and family incomes (C5).
- F4e: other currencies (C7, D-13).
- F5: invites, seat claiming, returning members (D-26). The privacy policy text is ready before F5 starts.
- F6: leaving, removal and detach; delete-my-data for family data; the published privacy policy; the demo family; family reports and the personal-report filter; removing old SharedExpense creation from the UI. With the demo family, revisit the demo's personal twin of a starter category merged into a family budget (accepted after the review of F4a's parts 5 and 6).
- After the F4e review (2026-10-01): F5 is invites and taking a seat (D-17, D-18). Returning members (D-26) and making another member an owner move to F6, next to leaving and removal. The privacy policy's draft is docs/family-budget/privacy-draft.md, which F6 publishes.
- After the F5 deploy (2026-10-01, decided by the PM): F6 is split in two. F6a is the membership's lifecycle (leaving, removal and detach, the rule back to equal shares, returning members, making another member an owner) and what "Delete all my data" shows of the family budgets. F6b is the privacy policy's publication, the demo family (H1), the family report (E1, and E3's check), H4, the owner's remarks on the interface, and the switcher's placeholder right after accepting an invite.
- After the F6a deploy (2026-10-02, decided by the PM): the owner confirmed F6a as built (ADR 0003 topic J), gaps 1, 2 and 4 to 9 of the F6a report; gap 3 is replaced by D-36. F6b, in this order: D-35 to D-37; the switcher's placeholder right after accepting an invite or leaving; the members table's actions at 375 px; then the privacy policy's publication, the demo family (H1), the family report (E1, and E3's check) and H4.
- After the OPS-1 deploy (2026-10-02, decided by the PM): F6 is split further. F6b: D-35 to D-39, the switcher's placeholder right after accepting an invite or leaving, the members table's actions at 375 px, and OPS-1's follow-ups (a read-only database role for the deploy scripts' checks, `deploy.sh adopt`). F6c, next: the privacy policy's publication, the demo family (H1), the family report (E1, and E3's check), H4 and the owner's remarks from the manual check.
- After the F6b deploy (2026-10-02): the owner confirmed F6b as built (ADR 0003 topic J, "F6b as built"), items 2 to 8 of the F6b report, and reviewed the privacy policy's draft (docs/family-budget/privacy-draft.md as of `7a60020`), which F6c publishes. F6c's plan, with the gaps it decides, is ADR 0003 topic J's "F6c plan".
- After the F6c deploy (2026-10-02, decided by the PM): the owner confirmed F6c as built (ADR 0003 topic J, "F6c as built"): the report's shape, the demo family's content, the twins merged into the demo family rather than into the family budget they come from, H4 for creation only with the API unchanged, the legal questions kept out of the published policy, and the mapping of F6b's items (D-35 to D-39) onto what was built. F7's way of switching: a command, `deploy.sh switch on|off`, never a hand edit of `.env`; the way back is `switch off`, which leaves the data and makes the family endpoints answer 404 again. F7's check in production: done with the owner's two test accounts, never his own account, whose personal ledger is kept for his Excel import; it ends with the family budget deleted (D-36) and "Delete all my data" for both test accounts.
- F7: the switch goes on in production, followed by a check with two real accounts.
  - Prerequisite: the client address of IPv6 clients (F6a's residual risk: with an AAAA record for app.finance-nl.com and Docker's IPv6 off, every IPv6 client could reach the api as one address) is settled before the switch goes on, so that D-29's per-address limit counts the browser's real address for them too. Settled by D-38 (2026-10-02): such an address is the bridge's gateway, a private one, for which only the per-user limit applies.
- After the F7 deploy (2026-10-02, decided by the PM): F7's deploy passed every check of `deploy.sh run`, but the privacy policy answered 403 (the deploy scripts' `umask 077` wrote the changed `privacy.html` with mode 600, which the web image kept and nginx couldn't read); `finish` recorded the browser checks as passed by mistake, the switch went on, and by D-41 it was switched off again. F7b, a stage of its own: the web image readable whatever the checkout's modes, with CI building it from a copy with the server's modes; page checks in the deploy scripts (`run`, `verify`, `finish`, `rollback.sh`), and `finish` recording the browser checks as failed, without asking, when a page fails; git commands that write the working tree under `umask 022`, everything else under 077; `switch`'s text. Its checks are `deploy/checks/F7b.sql`. F7's production check moves to F7b's checklist, after its `finish` and `switch on`. F7's report claimed that the `edge` network's subnet check proves D-29's per-address limit counts real clients; it doesn't (with Docker's IPv6 off, IPv6 clients arrive as the bridge's gateway, D-38's case), and the check is dropped from the checklists.
- After F7: the Excel import, with Family rows going into a family ledger.
- F8 (D-45, D-77): the multi-currency family ledger. F8a: the model and posting (schema V11, shares and records per currency, posting, balances and settlements, membership flows, the integrity check, the rollback guard), backend only. F8b: the interface, the report's screen, rates (D-47, D-49), D-53, D-54, the end-to-end suite and the deploy checklist. One deploy after F8a and F8b. F8c, before D3b: refunds (D-79), payments from counterparty accounts (D-80), the payer's payee (D-81) and the import's sync fields. Then D3b (the owner's import, D-82) and a deploy; D3c (the partner's claim and import, D-48) after it.
- After F8's deploy and F8c-0 (2026-10-07, decided by the PM, D-102): what D-77 called F8c is F8d. F8c is D-100 and D-101 (each user's time zone, V12), the error boundaries and guards of F8b-fix's finding (D-96), the five frontend tests that failed when a load was answered late, D-99 (random test order in CI) and the npm audit finding. F8d is refunds (D-79), payments from counterparty accounts (D-80), the payer's payee (D-81), the import's sync fields (D-48) and D-93, and comes before D3b.
- After F8c's deploy (2026-10-07, `b6258f6`, Flyway V12): F8d is built on `feature/family-budget` and not deployed: refunds (D-79), payments from counterparty accounts (D-80), the payer's payee (D-81), the import's sync fields (D-48), the journal's currencies (D-93), D-104 and D-107, with V13 and a rollback condition (D-116). Its choices, the agent's, are D-108 to D-116, for the PM to confirm. D3b follows it.
- After F8d's deploy (2026-10-07, `05397a7`, Flyway V13, decided by the PM): F8e is test hygiene and a clamp: D-117 (no implicit clock), D-118 (a leaving date never before the join date) and D-119 (tests read answers as parsed values), the time-zone spec's fixed titles, and F8d's deploy facts in the runbook; D-120 to D-124 record the owner's decision on the laptop's disk, the choices of F8e that the PM confirmed, and the rule for dispatching "CI on Ubuntu 26.04". No migration. D3b follows it.
- After F8e's deploy (2026-10-08, `39e6e61`, Flyway V13, decided by the PM): F8f records F8e's deploy facts and the host's first automatic reboot, decides D-125 to D-127 (reboots stay on, a scheduled shutdown stops a deploy, the deploy checklist's template), builds D-126 into the deploy scripts, and clears F8e's backlog: the end-to-end guards in CI (a step of the Frontend job, D-95), D-117's rule over `System.currentTimeMillis()`, `new Date()` and `ZoneId.systemDefault()`, and D-121's list of `Answers.rawText`'s callers. No migration, no application change. D3b follows it.
- After CI on F8f's M (`1eac505`, pushed 2026-10-12) failed in the step "End-to-end guards" (no browser on the runner), decided by the PM: F8f-fix, before F8f's deploy and before D3b. D-128 to D-133: the shutdown file's reading, the guards in both workflows, D-117's rule over `TimeZone.getDefault()`, D-126's window of 2 hours, a browser for the guards in CI, and CI's needs proven in a clean container. F8f is deployed as M′, F8f-fix's last commit. No migration, no application change.
- After F8f's deploy (2026-10-12, `fe35e26`, Flyway V13, decided by the PM, D-137): D3b is the owner's Excel import with the partner's family rows from his seat (D-82, D-136): the code, its tests, a local run on the real export (D-144) and a deploy, with V14 (D-148, D-150). D3b-run, the production import, follows as its own task after D3b's deploy is accepted; D3c (the partner's claim and his own import, D-48) after it.

D3b-run follows it.
- After D3b-accept-fix's deploy (2026-10-13, `1d3b811`, Flyway V14) and D3b-run (2026-10-13: the owner's history imported up to 2026-08-31, Passed, the runbook's "Deployed revisions"): D3b-doc records both and D-172 and D-173; D3c-0 surveys D3c, read only, with a local simulation of the partner's claim and his own import (data/private/reports/D3c-0.md). D3c-accept, a task of its own, runs the local run again on the export with the Excel fixes until no row is refused (the runbook's step 11.1); D3c-run, the production claim and import, is the runbook's step 11, after D3c's deploy is accepted.

## Later
Found along the way; not part of a stage yet.
- Postgres and Caddy patches (D-922, after OPS-4's rehearsal, 2026-11-16). Production pins Postgres 16.15 and Caddy 2.11.4. Newest on Docker Hub: Postgres 16.16 (2026-11-15), whose release notes list 43 CVEs, about half needing a feature this setup doesn't use (PL/Perl, PL/Tcl, dblink, postgres_fdw, subscriptions) and the rest needing a role that can run SQL (Keycloak's) or DDL rights: relevant only if Keycloak or its credentials were compromised, and a minor-version swap within 16 (no dump needed, a REINDEX is advised only for GIN, `pg_trgm` GiST and `bpchar` B-tree indexes, none in Keycloak's schema as far as read); Caddy 2.11.9 (2026-11-13), with security fixes in 2.11.6 (a `forward_auth`-plus-`reverse_proxy` wrong-connection fix, hop-by-hop headers stripped from 101 responses, constant-time sticky cookie hashes) and 2.11.9 (FastCGI header overrides, HTTP/2 `host` handling, a stricter TLS `client_auth` dropped when policies merge, go-jose 4.1.6): this setup uses plain `reverse_proxy` with automatic HTTPS, so the 2.11.6 reverse_proxy items are the ones that touch it. Neither was changed in OPS-4; each is its own window, rehearsed the same way (restore a dump into the new image for Postgres; `deploy/check-site.sh` and a throwaway Caddy for Caddy).
- `automation-cli` and the Admin API's audience check (D-934): the first use after OPS-4 may need the `kc-admin-api` scope on the client.
- Keycloak 26.8's other new defaults that this realm doesn't use (client-secret rotation, the SCIM API, organizations, `login-failures:v2` in the database) are not switched on or off by OPS-4; `scimApiEnabled` is `false` in both realms.
- D-370's cleanup (after UX-3): closed by D-381 (UX-4): both opening balances stay, and the owner clears his archived accounts' €0.01 in his own file.
- Sequential scans on posting. F2b's EXPLAIN showed the balance, integrity and rates statements reading all postings and hash-joining them with the ledger's entries, since posting has no ledger column; the same statements filtered by user_id had the same plan before F2b. Revisit after the Excel import, or when demo ledgers from open registration pile up. The options: a ledger_id on posting, checked by a trigger against its entry's; or statements that start from the ledger's entries and reach the postings through their index.
- Minimal transfers (called D3 in the F4b review). "Who owes whom" pairs the largest debtor with the largest creditor, then the next, as a display only; the model fixes each member's balance, not the transfers. The fewest transfers that settle every balance stay for later.
- The family paths (after the F4d review). The activity's paths `/family/{id}/expenses…` hold expenses, incomes and settlements; rename them to `/family/{id}/activity…`, with redirects from the old paths, which personal entries and the journal link to.
- Invites for a taken seat (after the F5 deploy). When an invite takes a seat, the other pending invites for that seat stay pending until they expire; accepting one answers 409 (D-29). Revoking them when the seat is taken stays for later.
- A long invite history. The owners' invite list keeps every invite, newest first; folding the old ones away stays for later.
- Making an owner a member again (gap 7 of the F6a report). Today an owner stops being one only by leaving or being removed; an endpoint that makes an owner a member again stays for later.
- One address for a household. The per-address rate limit of invites (D-29) counts every device behind one NAT together, such as a household's router.
- IPv6 in Docker (after the OPS-1 deploy, D-38). With Docker's IPv6 on for the `edge` network, IPv6 clients would reach Caddy with their own address, and the per-address limit would count them too. It changes the auth server's host, so it waits for a maintenance window.
- Separating a member's own entries from their shares in a family category's personal cash-flow line (after the F6c deploy, D-40).
- Settlement sides on an account that requires a counterparty, or on FX_EXCHANGE, in the settlement forms (D-162). Today only the import writes them (D-148, D-71).
- The dashboard's two columns from 80rem (D-609, after UX-14's review; D-593 amended when done), proposed for the next stage with code. Between 64 and 80rem the main column is too narrow for "Cash flow by category": at 1024 px its headers wrap into narrow columns, "Year earlier Nov 1 – 12, 2025" over five lines. Two columns from 80rem instead of 64rem; under 80rem one column, in the same order. `07-dashboard`'s step 13 follows (its 1024 px case then one column). Closed by D-654 (UX-16).
- The stale JSDoc above `rateSourceText` (D-609, after UX-14's review), proposed for the next stage with code: the comment of the removed `usedRateText` ("A rate a figure in the base currency used, in words …") stayed above `rateSourceText`'s own in `frontend/src/dashboard.ts`; remove it. Closed by D-654 (UX-16).
- The emails' plain link (D-639, after UX-15's review; D-626 amended when done), proposed for a later stage: drop the plain link under the button from auth_server's `email/html/template.ftl`. Keycloak's action links are about 800 characters and fill half the email; the plain-text part keeps the link. Done in UX-19 (D-819).
- The emails' footer (D-639, after UX-15's review), proposed for a later stage: "Sent by Finance Tracker's sign-in. If you didn't expect it, you can ignore it." repeats each body's own "ignore" sentence; a footer that only names the service. Done in UX-19 (D-819).
- The descriptions on "Select login method" (D-639, after UX-15's review), proposed for a later stage: at "Try Another Way" during a first sign-in through Google or GitHub into an existing account, each way's description is dimmer than `--muted` in Blue and Dark; a PatternFly variable isn't mapped to a token in auth_server's `login.css`. Done in UX-19 (D-822, D-823).
- A check of the Keycloak theme's tokens (D-639, after UX-15's review), proposed for a later stage: that auth_server's `tokens.css` still equals `frontend/src/index.css`'s tokens. D-625 copies them by hand, and nothing notices a difference.
- The theme in the privacy policy (D-639, after UX-15's review), proposed for a later stage: the policy doesn't say that the chosen theme is also kept in the browser's local storage (since UX-10); one phrase beside the session storage paragraph. Done in UX-19 (D-821, D-824).
- The Node of the laptop's `frontend/` (D-672, UX-16's second commit), proposed for a later stage: its default Node v22.19.0's npm 11.6.2 writes a lock that CI's npm refuses. A `.nvmrc` naming CI's Node (and `engines` in `package.json`) would make the laptop's npm CI's; not done in UX-16's second commit, whose scope leaves `package.json` alone. D-669's check stays either way. Done in UX-19 (D-820, D-826).
- The browser's other local storage in the privacy policy (after UX-19, D-824), proposed for a later stage: besides the theme, the app keeps in `localStorage` an invite's token for up to 7 days (`invite.ts`), the last paying account of the family forms (`FamilyExpenseForm.tsx`, `FamilySettlement.tsx`) and a dismissed time zone hint (`timeZone.ts`); the policy names none of them. D-818 kept UX-19 to the theme. Done in UX-19's second commit (D-828, D-829).
- The emails UX-19 didn't see (D-827), proposed for a later stage: the SMTP test email (the dev stack's master admin has no address) and an organisation invite (no organisation in the realm), both through the same `template.ftl`.
- Unmapped PatternFly colours in the login theme (D-822), proposed for a later stage: the scoped borders and backgrounds (`--BorderColor--dark-100`, `--light-100`, `--BackgroundColor--light-100`, `--dark-100`), the icons' (`--icon--Color--light`, `--dark`) and the disabled ones (`--disabled-color--100`, `--200`), measured on every page the way D-822 measured the descriptions.
- A reproducible api jar (D-835, UX-19's third commit), proposed for a later stage: `project.build.outputTimestamp` in `backend/pom.xml`, or a Dockerfile that copies only `src/main`, so that a test-only commit doesn't give the api a new content and end signed-in sessions at its deploy.
- A value after a marker past its bar's end (D-678, after UX-16's review), proposed for a later stage, the owner to decide once he has seen the chart on his own data: where a comparison's marker lies past its bar's end, the value follows the marker (D-650, D-658) and stands apart from its bar: on "Last 3 months" compared with the previous period, Oct 2026's income €2,540.00 stands after its comparison's marker, about 80 px past the bar. Kept by D-713 (UX-17-doc), the owner to decide: since UX-17 it shows on the demo's "Past year" right after the load and on the landing page's `demo-dashboard.webp` (on 2026-11-13's demo, Mar and Jul 2026's income values after the previous year's marker).
- The hover panel's "(—)" (D-678, after UX-16's review), proposed for a later stage, the owner to decide as above: when the comparison's month has no figure, the panel shows "Income (—)", as before UX-16; no change shown there instead. Closed by D-687 (UX-17): a comparison month with no figure in either series is left out of the panel, and a series without a figure shows "—" alone.
- `DemoLedger`'s Javadoc of `varied` and `grown` (D-712, after UX-17's review), for the next stage with code: it says the step is "of the year and month", but the month term is 0 modulo 7, so the step depends on the year (and each item's salt) only.
- The partner's workbook (D-752, R-NOV), before his next export: he puts the same IDs into his workbook as values (12883 to 12952 for the rows the PM numbered; his next new ID is 12953), and fixes the number format of DEBIT_CHANGE, CREDIT_CHANGE, FAMILY_EXP and FAMILY_INC, so that his own export carries the IDs and the exact values and needs no rebuild.
- Done by D-767 (IMP-1, 2026-11-14): the benefit check's period (D-758, R-NOV), before the next run whose file starts after a member's join date: `ImportService.memberBenefits` compares from the later of the join date and the file's first row date (D-256's period rule), with a test on a file that starts mid-history; until then such a file gives one "benefit rows … 0.00" warning per month before it (D-757).
- The server's disk (D-848, OPS-3-doc), before the alerts stage (failed backup, disk, memory): its use grew by about 2.5 GB in a week (Hetzner's backups of `auth-1`, 9.27 GB on 2026-11-09 to 11.84 GB on 2026-11-14), in step with UX-16 to UX-18's deploys; find what grows, likely old images.
- (Closed by OPS-6's checklist, D-945 to D-958.) Postgres 17.12 for the app's database (D-939, OPS-5): rehearsed on the dump of 2026-11-16 (no `REINDEX` needed, the 24 numbers identical), not deployed: `deploy.sh run`'s guard refuses a changed `postgres` block. The owner decides a package for `deploy.sh db-image` (D-939's design: the same major only, a backup and restore test, `up -d --no-deps postgres`, the api's reconnection tried, Flyway, the numbers and the stage's checks) and its own window; until then the pin stays 17.11.
- Decided by the owner on 2026-11-17 (recorded by OPS-6's first commit): D-939's `deploy.sh db-image` is built in the next package (OPS-6), applied in its own window, before December's import; the old images of the server (keycloak:26.7, postgres:16.15, caddy:2.11.4, hello-world and the untagged postgres and keycloak ones, about 1.6 GB) are removed by the checklist, never by a script (D-948).
- The server's disk (D-848, D-940, D-944): done by OPS-5's step 3.7 and `prune-cache`; the numbers of the checklist's pre-flight and of the disk after it go into the next batch's first commit, and the hypothesis (build cache from deploys' layers) is confirmed or rejected there. The alerts stage (failed backup, disk, memory) still follows.
- A V18 condition in `rollback.sh` (D-859, FX-1): refuse below V18 while CBR rows exist or the key holds `source`, naming the checklist's database step; until then that step is only in FX-1's checklist.
